## L 3

I would like to know how your father and brothers are doing, especially the Assistant Rector <term>Conrector: a high-ranking academic official or deputy headmaster</term>, whom I ask you to greet on my behalf <note>original: "salutiren"</note>; and also what people where you are think of the printed little book, and whether it is also being slandered? Among us, many hungry hearts rejoice over it. But the "supreme evil man" <note>A reference to Böhme’s primary antagonist, the pastor Gregor Richter, who led the persecution against him</note> does not find it to his taste, because it teaches repentance <term>Busse: the spiritual turning of the heart toward God</term> and prayer. See Epistle 51, questions 2 and 3; compare Epistle 54, question 4 and following; Epistle 55, question 3.

In my talent <note>Böhme uses "talent" in the biblical sense from the Parable of the Talents (Matthew 25), referring to the spiritual gift and duty entrusted to him by God</note>, as I have been able to write it down in simplicity, you will clearly see the way to this, especially in this enclosed little book, which is also a product of my talent and was sent to print only a few weeks ago; which I present to the gentleman in love, as my Christian fellow-member, and I exhort him to read through it often, for its virtue is called "the longer, the dearer" <note>original: "Je länger je lieber"; an idiom suggesting something that grows more precious over time</note>. In this little book, the gentleman will see a true, brief foundation which is certain, for the author has experienced it in practice <note>original: "in der Praxi"; Böhme emphasizes that his insights come from lived spiritual experience rather than academic theory</note>. See Epistle 55, question 10.

I have now written to Mr. Balzer Walter <note>A close friend and associate of Böhme who traveled extensively to disseminate his writings</note> at the Leipzig Fair <term>Leipziger Messe: one of the most important trade fairs in Europe, serving as a vital hub for the distribution of books and ideas</term>, and [sent] him the little book on the **Election of Grace** <note>original: "Gnadenw." The cause of such anger (in Richter <note>Referring to Pastor Gregor Richter, the chief clergyman of Görlitz and Böhme's fierce critic</note>) has been the published, printed little book *Of Repentance and True Resignation* <term>Gelaſſenheit: a central concept in Böhme's thought, meaning the total surrender of the self-will to God's will</term>, which has done much good. This has annoyed the **Pharisaic spirit** <note>original: "Phariſäiſchen Geiſt"; a biblical reference to religious leaders who prioritize outward rules over inner spirit</note>, that such a foundation should be revealed; he fears that such teaching and way of life might also be demanded of him. This is not to his taste, because he sits in the lust of the flesh and serves the idol **Bacchus** <note>original: "Bacho"; the Roman god of wine, used here to accuse Richter of drunkenness and worldly indulgence</note> in carnal pleasure.

But know this for your information: his slandering and lies have only publicized and revealed my little book, so that now almost everyone—nobility, scholars, and simple folk alike—desires to read it and holds it very dear. In a short time, this little book has echoed and spread through almost all of Europe and is greatly loved; even at the **Electoral Court of Saxony** <note>original: "Churfl. Hof Sachſen"</note> etc. <note>The reference "150. ep. J. 6." likely points to a specific collection of Böhme's epistles or a numbered entry in his records.</note>

I inform you that Satan is completely enraged within our "high priest" <note>original: "oberſten Prieſter"; a sarcastic reference to Richter</note> because of the printed little book. He has become like one raving and mad with cursing, slandering, lying, and murdering. I see clearly that this little book is entirely contrary to the Devil, and he would therefore gladly murder me. Because of his cruel persecution, I must now stand under the **Cross of Christ** and bear His mark <note>original: "Malzeichen"; Böhme views his suffering as a spiritual badge of honor, identifying him with the suffering of Jesus</note>, for the Devil is now pouring out his last poison.

<summary>Böhme describes how the hostile opposition from Pastor Gregor Richter has backfired, leading to the widespread popularity of his book *Of Repentance and True Resignation* throughout Europe and the Saxon court. He interprets his personal persecution as a spiritual battle, seeing himself as a bearer of the Cross against a "Pharisaic" ecclesiastical establishment.</summary>

## *Apology* against *Gregory Richter*, *Senior Pastor* at Görlitz.

The outcry that has reached you is nothing but a **Pharisaic slander** <note>Böhme frequently compared his orthodox Lutheran critics to the biblical Pharisees, implying they were obsessed with legalism rather than spiritual truth</note>, with a defamatory, lying **lampoon** <term>Pasquille: a public satirical or abusive writing, often posted anonymously</term> in the Latin language, written on a sheet of paper. Within it, Satan has exposed the Pharisaic heart; this has occurred through God’s permission so that people should learn to recognize and flee the poison of this Pharisaic heart. And I almost believe that the coarsest Devil **dictated** <note>original: "dictiret"</note> this same lampoon, for he has revealed his claws all too clearly within it, so that one sees plainly that he is a liar and a murderer; and it will serve as a warning to many people so that they will take better care of their souls. Against this, I have written a defense <note>Böhme’s *Apologia contra Gregorium Richter*</note>, which you can obtain along with the lampoon from Mr. Michael Ender <note>A bookseller and merchant who helped distribute Böhme’s works</note> in your location; he will willingly lend it to you, where you will see strange things which I cannot write to you here. 60th Epistle, paragraph 3.

As a weak earthly instrument, according to the outer man, I—being an untrained and unlearned man <note>Böhme frequently reminds his readers that he was a simple shoemaker by trade, not a university-educated theologian, to emphasize that his insights came from divine inspiration rather than academic study</note>—could initially grasp and convey these high works only poorly. ### CHAPTER XI.
# Why Jacob Böhme
## wrote, and was compelled to write.
->Of this he writes thus:<-

> Although this foundation and great mystery have remained hidden from the world, and even human flesh and blood cannot grasp or comprehend it, yet the **GOD** who made the world wishes now, at the end, to reveal Himself, and all great mysteries shall become manifest: to indicate that 1) the great day of **Revelation** and 2) final **Judgment** is now near, and is to be expected daily; on which day shall be restored what was lost through **Adam**, and on which the **Kingdom of Heaven** and the **Kingdom of the Devils** shall separate in this world. ...reveal God in highest simplicity, which no one can resist:

And may everyone lift up their eyes because their redemption draws near, and not strive after shameful greed, pride, and lavish feasting and finery, nor suppose that this is the best life, while they actually sit in the midst of hell in their arrogance, guarding Lucifer. This they will soon have to see with great terror, anguish, and eternal despair, along with mockery and shame. One sees a terrible example of this in the devils, who were once the most beautiful angels in heaven, as I will write and reveal shortly hereafter. I let God have His way; I cannot resist Him. <note>original: "Auror. c. 4." refers to Chapter 4 of Böhme's "Aurora," where he describes the nature of God and the fall of Lucifer.</note>

What is still hidden? The true teaching of Christ? No, but rather <term>Philosophy</term> <note>In Böhme’s 17th-century context, "Philosophy" (Philoſophia) refers to "Theosophy" or the study of divine wisdom within the natural world, rather than modern academic philosophy.</note>, and the deep ground of God, the heavenly being, the revelation of the creation of the angels, the revelation of the terrible fall of the devil—from which evil originates—the creation of this world, the deep ground and mystery of man and all creatures of this world, the <term>Last Judgment</term> <note>original: "jüngste Gerichte," referring to the final divine trial of humanity.</note>, and the transformation of this world, the mystery of the resurrection of the dead and of eternal life.

This will be [revealed] in the depths in great simplicity...

...arise; why not in the heights of <term>art</term> <note>In this 17th-century context, "art" (Kunst) refers to human intellectual skill, academic learning, or worldly cleverness.</note>? So that no one may boast that he has done it himself, and so that the Devil’s pride might be exposed and brought to nothing. Why does God do this? Out of His great love and mercy for all nations, and to indicate that the time of <term>Restoration</term> <note>original: "Wiederbringung." This refers to the "restitution of all things" (Apocatastasis), a belief that all of creation will eventually be returned to a state of perfection in God.</note> of what was lost is now at hand, when people will see and enjoy perfection, and walk in the pure, bright, and deep knowledge of God.

For the sake of the violent strife and battle, and for the sake of the zeal which we (humans and the devils) have with one another, this revelation has been given to me, as well as the powerful inner drive to set all of this down on paper.

...[might] follow; I do not know entirely, only that several future mysteries are being shown to me in the <term>depths</term> <note>original: "Tieffe." In Böhme’s mystical theology, the "depths" refer to the inner spiritual essence of God and the hidden workings of nature.</note>. *Aurora*, chapter 11, paragraph 76 and following.

I do not write this for my own praise, but so that the reader might know where my <term>knowledge</term> <note>original: "Wissenschafft." In the 17th century, this term referred to a deep, systematic understanding or divine insight rather than modern experimental science.</note> stands, so that he does not seek someone else in me whom I am not. Rather, I am as all people are who struggle in **Christ Jesus** our King for the crown of eternal joy, and who live in the hope of perfection. The beginning of this perfection is on the day of the <term>Resurrection</term> <note>original: "Aufferstehung."</note>, which is now shortly at hand. This is very easy to see in the circle of the <term>rising</term> <note>original: "Auffgangs." A reference to the dawn (Aurora) of divine light.</note> in the <term>flash</term> <note>original: "Blitze." For Böhme, the "flash" or "lightning-bolt" is a central concept representing the sudden breakthrough of divine light and joy out of the "dark fire" of spiritual longing.</note>, in which nature reveals itself as if the day were about to break.

Therefore, see to it that you are not found sleeping in your sins; truly. The wise will notice it, but the godless remain in their sins. They say: "What is the matter with this fool? When will he finish his dreaming?" This happens because they have fallen asleep in fleshly lusts; wake up! See what kind of a dream that will turn out to be.

I would also like to rest in my <term>gentleness</term> <note>original: "Sanfftmuth." Böhme often characterized himself as a simple, quiet man who was reluctantly thrust into the role of a prophet.</note> if I did not have to do this; but the **God** who made the world is far too strong for me. I am the work of His hands; He may place me wherever He wills.

M 2

[...a dev]il's spectacle it must be, yet my hope is in God for the future life; in that, I will take the risk and not resist the Spirit, Amen. *Aurora, chapter 11, paragraph 76 and following.*

The scholars have brought forth many and various monsters <note>original: "monstra." In this context, Böhme refers to strange, distorted theories or intellectual phantoms regarding the nature of evil.</note> concerning the beginning of sin and the origin of the Devil, and they have struggled over it. Each one thought he had the right end of the stick <note>original: "die Axt bey dem Stiel," literally "the axe by the handle." A German idiom meaning to have a firm grasp or correct understanding of a matter.</note>, and yet it remained completely hidden from them all until this day.

But because it now wishes to reveal itself completely, as if in a clear mirror, it is well to presume that the great day of God’s revelation is now at hand, when the <term>fierceness</term> <note>original: "Grimmigkeit." In Böhme’s cosmology, this is the harsh, consuming quality of the "First Principle" (wrath) before it is balanced and transformed by the light of Love.</note> and the ignited fire shall separate from the light.

Therefore, let no one make himself stone-blind, for the time of the <term>restoration</term> <note>original: "Widerbringung." This refers to the theological concept of *apokatastasis*, the ultimate return of all created things to their original, perfect state in God.</note> of what man has lost is now at hand. The **dawn** is breaking; it is time to wake up from sleep. *Aurora, chapter 13, paragraph 2 and following.*

Here you may truly open your eyes, for you will see the hidden things that have been concealed from all people since the beginning of the world: for you will see the murder-dens of the Devil, and the cruel sin, enmity, and destruction. *Aurora, chapter 16, paragraph 1; compare chapter 19, aphorism 77; chapter 22, aphorism 5 and following, aphorism 19 and following, aphorism 50, aphorism 69; chapter 23, aphorism 85.*

<summary>Böhme asserts that the mysteries of sin and the Devil, long misunderstood by scholars, are now being revealed as a sign of the approaching divine restoration. He urges the reader to wake from spiritual sleep to witness these hidden truths as the "dawn" of a new age begins.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Böhme’s defense of his writing. He shifts from discussing the "monsters" of scholarly theory to addressing the personal mockery he faces from those who view his mystical insights as mere "delusion" or a distraction from his worldly trade as a shoemaker.</meta>

->❀ (0) ❀<-

I find that the cleverest devil is set against me; he will stir up scoffers. They will say that I wish to scrutinize <note>original: "ausgrüblen." This term carries a negative connotation of prying or over-intellectualizing divine mysteries through human reason rather than spiritual revelation.</note> the Godhead through my own delusion.

Yes, dear scoffer, you are indeed an obedient son of the devil; you may rightly mock the children of God. Should I, by my own ability, be able to fathom the Godhead so deeply if the Godhead did not ground itself in me? But do you also think that I am strong enough to resist <insert>this divine impulse</insert>?

Yes, dear proud man, the Godhead is also a completely gentle, simple, and still being; it does not brood in the depths of hell and death, but in its heaven. There, nothing exists but unanimous <term>gentleness</term> <note>original: "Sanftmuth." For Böhme, this is the essential quality of the "Light World" or the Second Principle of God’s being, characterized by peace and love.</note>; therefore, it would not be proper for me to do such a thing <note>meaning: to probe these depths by his own ego or pride.</note>.

But look, I did not bring this onto the path <note>original: "auff die Bahn bracht," an idiom meaning to initiate something or bring it into public discourse.</note>, but rather your own desire and high-exalted pleasure—these have moved the Godhead to reveal the desire of your heart to you in the highest simplicity and greatest depth, so that it may be a witness against you and a proclamation of the earnest Day of God; this I say as a word of the earnest God. **Aurora, chapter 24, paragraph 74 and following; compare chapter 15, aphorism 3, 4.**

I know full well that the children of the flesh will mock me and say that I should attend to my own calling <note>original: "Beruff." This is a pointed reference to Böhme's critics who argued that as a common shoemaker, he had no right to write on theological matters.</note>, and [concern] myself with thi—

<summary>Böhme defends his writing as a divine mandate rather than personal intellectual vanity, arguing that his insights are a response to the spiritual state of his era. He rebukes scoffers who believe he should stick to his trade as a shoemaker instead of exploring the depths of the Godhead.</summary>
<keywords>scoffers, Godhead, gentleness, divine revelation, vocation, Aurora, spiritual resistance</keywords>

[the]se things alone and unconcerned, and rather apply myself more diligently to that which fills the belly for me and mine, and let those <term>philosophize</term> <note>In Böhme’s time, "philosophizing" referred to the deep investigation into the nature of God and the universe, not just academic logic.</note> who have studied it and are called to do so.

For when I thought of the "belly" <note>A reference to his physical needs and his work as a craftsman/shoemaker.</note> and decided within myself to abandon this my undertaking, then the gates of heaven were bolted shut within my <term>insight</term> <note>original: "Erkantnis." By this I understand that the Spirit must be proven through the <term>cross</term> and <term>tribulation</term> <note>These are traditional terms for the suffering and trials a Christian must endure to grow spiritually.</note>; likewise, I have not lacked for physical temptation, but have always had to stand in the <term>struggle</term> <note>original: "Kampff." This refers to the "spiritual warfare" Böhme felt he was waging against demonic influences.</note>, so greatly has the devil been set against it.

<summary>Böhme describes his internal struggle between his worldly duties as a shoemaker and his spiritual mandate to write, concluding that divine insight is only maintained through suffering and spiritual battle.</summary>

Because it is God’s eternal will and decree to do us good, and to reveal His <term>mysteries</term> <note>original: "Geheimnisse." A central Böhme concept; the idea that the natural world is a "mirror" or "signature" through which God's hidden essence can be understood by the enlightened mind.</note> *The Three Principles, chapter 16, section 2.*

Dear <term>Soul</term> <note>original: "Gemüthe." It is truly time to wake from sleep, for the <term>Bridegroom</term> <note>A reference to Christ’s return, based on the Parable of the Ten Virgins (Matthew 25), signaling a time of spiritual urgency.</note> comes. *The Three Principles, chapter <unclear>14.</unclear> section 120.*

Because in the <term>new rebirth</term> <note>original: "neuen Widergeburt." The core of Böhme's practical theology, describing the internal transformation and renewal of a person through divine grace.</note> there is the highest and greatest love / not only toward God, or toward oneself, but toward other people, one's brothers and sisters / so that those same newborn souls have directed their desire and love toward mankind / and have taught quite earnestly with gentleness and correction: so that they have also, for the sake of their love in their teaching, willingly given their lives unto death.

...to labor and to enjoy the growth of what he has sown; therefore my hand shall not cease to dig—we say this without jest—as one recognized as highly precious in the wonders of God, in the counsel of the noble <term>Virgin</term> <note>original: "Jungfrauen." These many deep and difficult matters are of no use to you; you should not gape at them. We must only set them down so that you may see what the <term>foundation</term> <note>original: "Grund." ...come to this end, so that you may see how you act so blindly without <term>knowledge</term> <note>original: "Erkäntniß." You persecute one another, revile and disgrace one another; you stir up war and rebellion, devastating land and people for the sake of the true knowledge of **God** and His will, and yet you are as blind to **God** as a stone. You do not know yourselves; instead, you are so frenzied that you fight over **God**, who is the Maker, Sustainer, and Supporter of all things, and who is the *center* <note>original: "Centrum." Thus, you fight over His light, which never appears in wrath and malice for all eternity; rather, His *center* rises in gentle love and humility. And you are so frenzied and mad, believing that you hold it on your tongues in the conflict of malice. You do not have it; rather, you have the *history* <note>original: "Historiam." Here, Böhme uses the term to mean "dead history" or "mere narrative"—the outward story of faith that lacks the internal, living spirit.</note> of the Saints who had the light shining from their *center*; therefore, they spoke from the **Holy Spirit**, who proceeds from the light. But you take their words while the *center* of your heart is firmly closed; you run and race in the four forms of malice (which are <term>pride</term> <note>original: "Hoffart."</note>, <term>greed</term> <note>original: "Geitz."</note>, <term>envy</term> <note>original: "Neid."</note>, and <term>wrath</term> <note>original: "Zorn."</note>). Böhme’s extensive commentary on the book of Genesis.</note> chapter 21, aphorism 17. <term>On the Election of Grace</term> <note>original: "Gnadenw." or *Von der Gnadenwahl*. Who is the cause for the <term>Center</term> <note>original: "Centrum." In Böhme's theosophy, the "Center" or "Ground" refers to the foundational point where the divine essence begins to manifest into being.</note> or Ground of the Holy Trinity revealing itself? It is precisely human seeking; their desire. People are now fighting over Christ’s person; likewise, over the <term>election of God</term> <note>original: "Wahl Gottes." Refers to the heated 17th-century theological debates regarding predestination—who God chooses for salvation.</note>, and because of it they provoke war and reviling. God’s love no longer wants this, and reveals itself even now so that man might see what he is doing, and leave the strife over God’s will to enter into a truly Apostolic life, and no longer engage in <term>intellectual squabbles</term> <note>original: "Wissen-zancken." Literally "knowledge-quarrels"; the bickering of academics and theologians over dogma rather than living faith.</note>; as has happened for a long time in <term>Babel</term> <note>original: "Babel." For Böhme, Babel represents the "city of confusion"—the corrupt, institutionalized church systems that value form and argument over the spirit.</note>.

But that a <term>simple mouth</term> <note>original: "einfältiger Mund." Böhme frequently identifies as a "simple" uneducated layman (a shoemaker by trade) to contrast his divinely inspired insights with the "high learning" of university theologians.</note> must now speak such great things, and not a highly learned one—ask God about that, why He does it. If a shepherd, as David was, were to become a king by God’s command today, the world would not believe it either until he sat in royal honors.

Yet Christ came in humble form, and His apostles were only lowly people; God can still do such a thing, so that He may bring to nothing the "self-reason" of this world: I tell you, we speak what we know, and testify to what we see. *Apology against <insert>Balthasar</insert> Tilke*, aphorisms 640, 643. Compare *Reflections on Esaias Stiefel's Booklet*, aphorism 6 and following.

Although I, as a simple man, never intended in all my days to converse with such high-ranking people using my gift—which was given to me by God out of His love and grace—or to become known among them through it: rather, after the <term>high light</term> <note>original: "hohe Licht." A reference to a sudden, profound spiritual illumination or "awakening" Böhme experienced.</note> was ignited within me and the <term>fiery impulse</term> <note>original: "feurige Trieb." The inner spiritual drive or "burning" necessity to communicate his visions.</note> overcame me, it was my will alone to write down what I actually saw and recognized in the spirit, and to keep my writings to myself.

I saw well what was to happen in the future, but that I

...fought the evil nature and its influences, and often the <term>Devil's deceit</term> <note>Böhme frequently writes about the internal struggle between divine light and the deceptive "self-will" influenced by demonic forces.</note> and <term>God’s wrath</term> <note>original: "Gottes Zorn." During this, many a storm was waged against reason, and against flesh and blood, and the Devil. My noble heart, please do not take it as a joke, what God reveals to us out of his love. Do not look upon the **simplicity** of the man through whom He does such things; for it is well-pleasing to Him to reveal His power through the weak and the foolish, as the world regards them. It happens as a lesson to the world, because everyone lives in <term>strife</term> <note>original: "Zanck." A key Böhmean term referring to the core point of origin from which all life and divine essence flow.</note> of His being and of all beings is revealed to them. This all happens out of His love toward us, so that we might finally depart from this miserable conflict and quarrel and enter into a brotherly and childlike love. 17th epistle, aphorism 7.

It stands written: *They shall all be taught by God, and recognize the Lord.* Also: *I will pour out my Spirit over all flesh.* Item: *Your sons and daughters shall prophesy, and your young men shall have visions.*

Why then would one mock if **GOD** poured out His Spirit over such a simple man, so that he must write beyond the reach of all human reason, higher than the foundation of this world is?

Dear Sir, it happens out of God's love toward you, so that you may yet see the ground and root of your **scholastic disputes**; for many have sought, but not...

I have not set this down to vex Mr. <term>B. Tilke</term> <note>Balthasar Tilke (1560–1628) was a Silesian official and contemporary critic who challenged Böhme’s unorthodox theological views.</note> nor anyone else, but because of the long-standing disunity of reason, in which the soul wanders astray and the truth lies hidden, while in this article concerning God’s will, reason thus judges and runs without foundation. 40th epistle, aphorism [...]

And although I am hated by many in the world for this reason, it will yet be seen very soon why God has opened the <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: "Mysterium magnum." A foundational concept in Böhme’s philosophy, referring to the primordial "abyss" or the spiritual womb from which all existence originates.</note> as the foundation of all secrets to a layman and a simple man. I cannot yet reveal all things that are known to me, though it might well happen among worthy people if I find it to be God’s will and good for mankind; as just recently a very noble <term>little pearl</term> <note>original: "Perlein." A common mystical metaphor for a moment of profound spiritual enlightenment or the discovery of the divine spark within.</note> was revealed to me, which has its time for actual usefulness, but is useful to me in my soul at every hour. You should not wonder so greatly at the **simplicity** <note>original: "Einfalt." Böhme emphasizes his lack of formal academic training, viewing his "simplicity" as the reason God chose him to shame the "learned" and the "proud."</note> of what God does, for the time of **pride** has come to an end. 40th epistle, aphorisms 16, 17.

# CHAPTER XII.
## Jacob Böhme’s writings were not made known immediately.

My writings have their own gifts and will stand in their own time, for they have a very precious, earnest birth and origin. 2nd Epistle, aphorism 7.

One should not at first strew pearls upon the road, for they are indeed precious; rather, one must wait for another time until they become common, so that the <term>driver</term> <note>original: "Treiber." This likely refers to a taskmaster or worldly authority—those who drive others for profit or power—who would exploit or trample spiritual truths.</note> does not swallow them up. 4th Epistle, aphorism 36.

I ask that you act wisely with my writings, and also keep my name secret until the dark night finally arrives, as it has been shown to me; then shall the <term>little pearl</term> <note>original: "Perlein." Böhme frequently uses the "pearl" as a metaphor for the spark of divine wisdom or the "Virgin Sophia" hidden within the soul.</note> be found. For as long as my beloved is sated, he slumbers and lies in the sleep of this world; but when the Lord shall awaken him with the storm wind and they stand in anguish, then they shall cry out fearfully to the Lord and rouse from sleep; then these writings shall stand, and the pearl shall be sought within them.

I also ask and desire that no one trouble themselves with printing [these works] against my will, for that shall only happen after the <term>tempest</term> <note>original: "Ungewitter." Böhme often predicted a period of great social and religious upheaval—a "storm"—after which his spiritual insights would be truly valued.</note>. 7th Epistle, aphorism 7.

# CHAPTER XIII.
## Jacob Böhme’s writings have been greatly persecuted, and why.

### Concerning the <term>territory</term> <note>original: "Revier." A term used by Böhme to describe the jurisdictional space or "district" of a spiritual power.</note>, place, and dwelling, as well as the governance of the angels, how it stood in the beginning after the Creation, and how it came to be so.

Here the Devil will defend himself like a biting dog, for his shame shall be uncovered here; he will give the reader many a hard blow and constantly cast it into doubt, [claiming] it is not so. For nothing pains him more than when—

He also warns that his revelations regarding the fallen state of the Devil will provoke fierce spiritual opposition.</summary>

...one reproaches him for his glory <note>original: "Herligkeit." Now, if a reader should come upon this chapter in whom the fire of the Holy Spirit is somewhat weak, I truly fear the Devil will beset him and provoke him to doubt whether things are as they are written—so that his kingdom does not stand so completely exposed and his shame is not so entirely uncovered. If he can only bring this to doubt in one heart, he will not lack for his skill, effort, and labor; I already see beforehand that he has this in mind. Therefore, I want to warn the reader to read it with diligence and to remain patient until he comes to the creation and the <term>regiments</term> <note>original: "Regimente." I have placed my confidence in the strong God and will venture it, and see what will come of it. This refers to "Gnosis" or a direct, experiential realization of divine truths rather than mere book-learning.</note> of my God remains with me; then I have enough, both here and there <note>Meaning in both this earthly life and the eternal life to come.</note>.

> And though my body and soul should languish, yet You, God, are my confidence, my salvation, and the comfort of my heart. Psalm 73:26.

Just as gold must be tested by fire, so it has happened to this hand <note>Likely referring to the writings of Jakob Böhme or the hand of the author himself.</note>: It did not lack for **putrefaction** <note>original: "Putrefaction." In alchemy, this is the stage of decay or "blackness" that must occur before spiritual purification and transformation can happen.</note>. Everyone wanted to trample the simple child underfoot when a small wreath was first placed upon him. Oh, what great trouble the Devil took to see if he might defile it! How busy he was! When I reflect on it, I can only wonder greatly and rightly thank GOD, who has preserved me. Oh, how he strived to tear that little wreath apart! How he incited the Antichrist <note>In this context, "Antichrist" refers to the established religious authorities who Böhme believed had lost the true spirit of Christ.</note> to persecute this hand, so that everyone should view it as an abomination.

But it went for the Devil just as it did with Christ, when he set the Pharisaic Antichrist against Him so that they crucified Christ: then the Devil thought, "He is gone now; I shall surely have peace from His teaching, which has destroyed my kingdom." So it is here as well. But he only stirred up the first storm by doing so: Christ stormed Hell for him and took him captive in His wrath. Likewise, through this hand, his **smoke-hole** <note>original: "Rauchloch." A vivid metaphor for a vent or opening through which the Devil's influence or "smoke" is exposed and attacked.</note> is first being opened, which he will not be able to close again until his judgment. We write this to the reader as an example.
->*The Threefold Life*, Chapter 12, Aphorisms 42, 43<-

**Pasquill** <note>A "Pasquill" (or pasquinade) is a satirical lampoon or a published attack. Here, the author reproduces the mocking voice of his critics.</note>: If you now recognize the **Shoemaker** <note>original: "Schuster." Jakob Böhme was a shoemaker by trade, a fact his academic critics used to mock him as uneducated.</note> by his claws, as one recognizes a lion, would you then read his books? Rather, avoid such things as the Devil’s dung and a cruel error, and stick simply to the Word of God; and beware of the Shoemaker’s students, whether they be noble or doctor, tailor, man, or woman. Lord Christ, steer the tools of Satan, and do not let Your word be darkened. Yes, yes, yes—oh, let it happen, Amen!

**Answer**: It is well to note in these words...

...why this lampoon <note>original: "Paßqvill"; a libelous or mocking piece of writing used here to describe the published attacks against Böhme.</note> was written; namely, so that he <note>The Devil, acting through Böhme's critics.</note> might destroy and utterly root out me and my writings through such defamatory insults. The Devil is afraid that my writings are opening up his **smoke-hole** <note>original: "Rauchloch." Böhme uses this metaphor to suggest his work exposes the "stink" of Hell or the secret ways the Devil influences the world.</note>; therefore, he paints me in such colors that no one shall read them. Otherwise, people might repent, and thus his kingdom would suffer a great loss.

But this Devil is far too coarse; he goes about too nakedly, so that people might notice him and learn to know him. For one sees his claws too clearly in his negligence and blasphemy, showing that he is an entirely poisonous spirit. It is now quite a different time; people no longer believe everything everyone says so quickly—they want to see proof. Why does he not prove it from the printed booklet, since he forbids it to the congregation? Such defamatory blasphemy is no proof; one could also call it filth <note>original: "Koth," meaning dung or excrement.</note>. *Apology against Richter, page 363.* <note>Reference to Böhme's "Apologia gegen Balthasar Richter," a defense against the Primarius of Görlitz who persecuted him.</note>

Though I may not receive great thanks in my time from those who love their bellies more than the Kingdom of Heaven, my writings nonetheless have their own gifts and stand in their own time. For they have an exceedingly precious and earnest birth and origin; and when I reflect on my own minor, low, and simple person, I wonder at them even more than my opponent does. *2nd Epistle, Aphorism 7.*

I do not doubt that Your Grace <note>original: "E. G." (Eure Gnaden); a formal address to a patron or person of high social rank.</note> will yet receive a most beautiful wreath from the Virgin of the Eternal Wisdom of God <note>In Böhme’s mystical theology, "Sophia" or Divine Wisdom is personified as a heavenly Virgin who leads the soul to God.</note> for this. When that happens, it will be dearer to Your Grace than all temporal goods and this entire world with all its nature and splendor—of which I would not wish to write if I had no knowledge <note>original: "Erkantnuß," a term for deep, experiential spiritual understanding.</note> of it.

For otherwise, among the wise of this world—who out of their own pride, without God's Spirit, attribute all knowledge and science to themselves alone—I am but a poor... <meta>The text breaks off here, continuing the comparison between worldly wisdom and spiritual revelation on the following page.</meta>

...receive no thanks, but only mockery, in which I nonetheless greatly rejoice, to bear shame for the sake of the name and knowledge <term>Erkäntnüß|In Böhme’s vocabulary, this refers to an experiential, mystical understanding of God rather than mere book learning.</term> of God. For if my knowledge were born from their schools, they would love their own; but because it is from another school, they do not recognize it. Therefore they despise it, just as they did to all the prophets, and even to Christ and his apostles. I will not let this lead me astray, but as I have begun, I will cling to my GOTT and Creator with all the greater earnestness, and surrender myself to Him, let Him do in me what He wills.

I attribute no cleverness to myself, nor do I rely on any purpose of reason <term>Vernunfft|Human logic or worldly intellect, which Böhme often contrasts with divine revelation.</term>; for I see and find quite clearly and brightly that God walks a very different path.

Therefore, if we proceed like children, and not in our reason, but cling to him only with longing and true earnestness, and place all our trust in him, we shall sooner attain the noble Virgin of His Wisdom <note>The "Noble Virgin" (Sophia) is a central figure in Böhme’s mysticism, representing Divine Wisdom that reveals the mysteries of the universe to the seeker.</note> than in our own sharp lights; for when she comes, she brings with her true wisdom and heavenly understanding, and without her I know nothing. *5th Epistle, Aphorism 4.*

Something very high has indeed begun with a new book, yet I have been powerfully resisted by the Prince of Wrath <note>original: "Fürsten des Grimmes." Böhme uses this term to describe the dark, destructive force within nature, often personified as the Devil.</note>; thus the same has been hindered until this day. I hope that it shall be written within a short time, for it is an herb that the Devil will not find tasty; yet the Lord's will must prevail. *7th Epistle, Aphorism 10.*

To speak reasonably of these things, we see it before our eyes that this path is entirely contrary to the Devil, because he rages against it without cause. It is almost a great wonder that he creates such a clamor against such a small little prayer-book <note>Böhme is likely referring to his work "The Way to Christ," which focused on personal piety and drew the ire of local clergy.</note>; it must certainly taste bad and stink to him, whereas many large books full of foolery, and partly also full of su-

For he thinks to build his kingdom strongly now, but sees that it is starting to become full of holes <note>original: "lückicht"</note>; for the demolishers are now arriving in many places, especially from the North <term>Mitternacht|Literally "midnight," but in the symbolic geography of 17th-century mysticism, it refers to the North (specifically Northern Europe) as the source of spiritual reformation and light.</term>. # CHAPTER XIV.

## Jacob Böhme’s writings cannot be understood by everyone, and why.

I wish to have the reader faithfully warned, as if with a preface to this great mystery: if he does not understand these things, yet would gladly understand them, let him pray to God for His Holy Spirit, that He might enlighten him with the same.

Without the enlightenment of the Spirit, you will not understand this mystery; for in the human spirit there is a strong lock against it, which must first be opened: and no human being can do this, for the Holy Spirit alone is the key to it.

Therefore, if you wish to have an open gate <note>original: "Porte"</note> into the Godhead, you must walk <note>original: "wallen" — this suggests a flowing, pilgrim-like movement or a state of being moved by a higher power.</note> in God’s love; I have placed this here for your instruction. *Aurora, Chapter 13, Aphorism 26 and following.*

Should it happen that these writings are read, and perhaps the Sodomitic world <note>Böhme uses "Sodomitic" to describe a society he viewed as morally corrupt, materialistic, and spiritually blind.</note> and its fattened swine <note>original: "Mast-Säue" — a derogatory term for those who indulge in worldly pleasures and academic arrogance at the expense of spiritual growth.</note> come upon them and root around in my pleasure garden—those who can do, know, or understand nothing but to blaspheme and haughtily mock, knowing neither themselves nor, much less, God's children—then I will have written nothing for them. I close my book with a firm wall and a bolt against such idiots and wild calves of the Devil, who sit only in the Devil’s death-pits up to...

<summary>Böhme warns that his mystical writings are inaccessible to the intellectually arrogant and can only be understood through the enlightenment of the Holy Spirit. He characterizes his critics as spiritually blind and predicts the eventual decline of the Devil's influence through a spiritual awakening from the North.</summary>

<keywords>Holy Spirit, Enlightenment, Spiritual Mystery, North (Midnight), Aurora, Divine Love, Resistance to Truth</keywords>

...over the ears, and they do not know themselves; they do exactly what the Devil, their schoolmaster, does, and remain children of the fierce wrath of God. But I wish to have written clearly enough here for the children of God; let the world and the Devil rage and storm even into the abyss, for their hourglass is set up. Everyone will reap what they have sown and will notice the hellish fire—in which they have no faith here—tickling them well for their proud, contemptuous arrogance.

Also, I cannot well neglect to write this down, since God will demand an account of everyone's gifts—how they have invested them—for He will demand His entrusted pound <note>A reference to the Parable of the Talents/Pounds in Luke 19, where a master expects his servants to invest the money given to them.</note> with interest <note>original: "Wucher" — here meaning increase or fruitfulness rather than predatory lending</note> and give it to the one who has gained much. But since I can do no more for Him at this time, I let His will prevail and continue to write according to my knowledge.

Regarding the children of God, they will well perceive what my writing is, for it is a truly excellent testimony; it can be proven with all creatures and all things, especially in Man, who is God’s image and likeness. But to the children of wickedness, it remains hidden and is a firm seal against them. Although the Devil would like to smell the roast <note>A German idiom meaning to "get wind" of a secret or sense a plan in motion.</note> and stir up the stormy weather from the East toward the North <term>Mitternacht|Literally "midnight," but used here to mean the North, which Böhme often associates with the source of spiritual light and reformation.</term>, where then a lily <note>In Böhme’s symbolism, the Lily represents the blossoming of the new spiritual life or the "Age of the Lily."</note> grows in the fierce tree with one root, as wide as the tree reaches with its branches, bringing its fragrance even into the Paradise of God.

A time will come that is wondrous; but as it begins in the night, many will not see it because of sleep and the great darkness. However, the sun will shine for the children in the middle of the night. *Preface to the Three Principles, aphorism 18 and following.* <note>original: "seqq." — an abbreviation for the Latin *sequentes*, meaning "and following."</note>

The time of revelation and the spewing out of the Beast <note>A reference to the apocalyptic Beast from the Book of Revelation.</note> is coming, of which to the children of hope...

...of love may be sufficiently understood herein? But for the servants of the Antichrist, there is a wall and a seal against it, until the wrath of whoredom <note>original: "Hurerey" — Böhme uses this biblical term to describe spiritual infidelity or the corruption of the church through worldly power.</note> is fulfilled over them, and Babel, the whore of the great beast, is repaid, and she disgraces the crown of her former kingdom, and the eyes of the blind are opened; then she sits as a shameful whore whom everyone judges to damnation. *The Three Principles, Chapter 4, Aphorism 22.*

There is a great death in the understanding. Although I write clearly enough in German <note>original: "teutsch" — the author emphasizes writing in the common tongue rather than scholarly Latin to make these mysteries accessible to the layperson.</note>, I will nonetheless remain silent to that same death in the uncomprehending soul; I cannot help that. It is said: *You must be born again if you wish to see the Kingdom of God.* If you wish to know this, then lay aside the little hat of pride in your mind and walk with us into the paradisiacal rose-garden; there you will find a herb growing, and if you eat of it, your eyes will be opened so that you recognize and see what Moses has written.

The commentaries <note>original: "Glossen" — marginal notes or scholarly interpretations that Böhme often felt obscured the true spiritual meaning of the Bible.</note> that have been placed over Moses out of human reason will not show you Paradise, much less the Creator. The Prophet and Apostle learned more in one hour in the School of Paradise than the Doctor in his school in thirty years. Nothing depends on one’s own wit; to whom God wills, He gives it for free; it cannot be bought for any money or favor; King Solomon will tell you that. *The Three Principles, Chapter 8, Aphorism 36 and following.*

Because man is of such tough comprehension, we might not yet be sufficiently understood; therefore, we wish to set down once more, briefly and properly, how these great mysteries are. For we know what kind of adversary we have—namely, the Prince of this World <note>A traditional title for Satan.</note>—who will not sleep if he could suppress this noble grain of mustard seed <note>Refers to the Parable of the Mustard Seed, representing the small beginning of divine grace that grows into a great spiritual tree.</note>. *The Three Principles, Chapter 22, Aphorism 57.*

The simple man is almost beyond comprehension, so we will deal with him like a child, in the hope that he might become seeing and find the pearl <note>The "Pearl of Great Price," a metaphor for divine wisdom or the kingdom of heaven.</note>. For not everyone will find what we have found in God’s love, although we would gladly grant it to all.

There is a great death within the understanding. Although I write clearly enough <note>original: "teutsch genug" — literally "German enough," a common idiom meaning to speak plainly or candidly.</note>, I will still remain mute to that same death in the uncomprehending soul; for that, I am not to blame. For we know what sort of adversary we have—namely, the Prince of this World <note>A biblical title for the Devil (John 12:31).</note>—who will not sleep if he could suppress this noble mustard seed <note>The "mustard seed" refers to the small, internal beginning of the Kingdom of God within the human soul.</note>. <note>Reference to *The Three Principles*, chapter 22, aphorism 57.</note>

The simple person is almost beyond reaching <note>Böhme suggests that the unlearned or "simple" struggle to grasp these depths without divine help.</note>, so we will deal with him in a childlike manner, to see if he might become seeing and find the pearl <note>The "Pearl of Great Price" (Matthew 13), representing the indwelling Spirit of Christ or Divine Wisdom.</note>. For not everyone will find what we have found in God's love, although we would gladly grant it to all.

He contrasts the immediate revelation of the "School of Paradise" with the slow, often fruitless learning of worldly scholars.</summary>

--- Page 244 ---
<meta>This page continues the introduction to Jakob Böhme’s mystical theology. Böhme emphasizes that his writings cannot be understood through traditional academic study or "historical" knowledge, but only through a spiritual "new birth" or internal transformation. He contrasts the "bloated kingdom" of the worldly mind with the fragrance of the spiritual "lily."</meta>

<page-num>(0)</page-num>
<meta>catchword: se-</meta>

There is indeed a great gap between them, such as the bloated kingdom of this world with the Devil; he will defend himself like a biting dog, though the scent of the lily <note>original: "Lilien-ruch" — the fragrance of the spiritual lily, Böhme's symbol for the flowering of the soul in Christ's love</note> makes him powerless; thus we will now speak in a childlike manner. <note>Reference to *The Three Principles of the Divine Essence*, chapter 23, aphorism 36.</note>

There is no need for much agonizing or heavy thinking over our deep writings; we write from another *Principle* <note>original: "Principio"</note>. No reader understands us correctly at the root unless his mind is born in God. No *historical* science <note>original: "Historische Wissenschafft" — refers to knowledge based on external facts or tradition rather than internal experience</note> should be sought in our writings. For just as it is not possible to behold God with earthly eyes, it is likewise impossible for an unenlightened mind to grasp heavenly thoughts and senses within the earthly vessel; like must only be grasped by like.

Indeed, we carry the heavenly treasure in an earthly vessel, but there must be a heavenly container hidden within the earthly one, otherwise the heavenly treasure will not be seized or preserved. No one should think that he will find the lilies of the heavenly plant through deep searching and pondering, unless he enters into the new birth through earnest repentance so that it grows within himself. Otherwise, it is merely a *history* <note>original: "Historia"</note> where his mind never experiences the ground <term>ground: "Grund," the underlying essence or foundation of reality</term>, yet he imagines he has grasped it; but his mind makes it manifest what spirit's child it is. For it is written: They are taught by God. <note>Reference to *The Three Principles*, appendix, aphorism 30, point 3.</note>

We wish to set the ground of the eternal bond as a mirror for those who desire to see; however, it is true that he cannot learn it from us unless he himself enters into the new birth, into the life of Jesus Christ, so that the Divine Light itself shines within him; otherwise, we remain merely historical to him and are not understood. <note>Reference to *The Threefold Life of Man*, chapter 1, aphorism 25.</note>

We would faithfully admonish the reader not to seek our meaning in the wisdom of this world, but in the light of eternal nature <term>eternal nature: the spiritual reality or "birth-cycle" from which the material world emerges</term>, to which we would also direct him, namely into the new rebirth, into the life of Christ. Otherwise, we are mute and misunderstood to him, and he might as well leave these writings unmastered if he remains outside of this.

<summary>Böhme argues that spiritual truth cannot be attained through intellectual study ("history") but requires a "new birth" in God to transform the seeker's mind. He warns that without this internal light, his deep writings will remain incomprehensible and "mute" to the reader.</summary>
<keywords>new birth, spiritual illumination, earthly vessel, heavenly treasure, eternal nature, Jacob Böhme, inner experience vs historical knowledge</keywords>

<page-num>(0)</page-num>
<sig>A2</sig>
<meta>catchword: angelic</meta>

taste, or will eat of the food of the first center <note>original: "centri" — in Böhme's thought, the "center" refers to the foundational source of life or the "wheel of nature"</note>, and his mockery will gnaw at him in the fire of his own life.

We wish to grant him the light for whose sake this hand has written down these deep mysteries <note>original: "Geheimniß"</note>; not for its own benefit, which it already possesses, but for the sake of the lilies and the angelic <note>original: "Englischen" — this refers to the realm of angels (*Engeln*), not the nation of England</note> world.

Pay close attention here; you will see what you have not seen since the heavy fall of Adam. Consider what this means and what is revealed here: do not step in the footsteps of the proud Pharisees who crucified Christ and remained blind to the light, otherwise the same will happen to you.

Look not at the hand of this pen <note>A humble reference to the author as a mere instrument of God</note>, for it can do nothing, but look at the center <note>original: "centrum"</note> from which the light shines. It shines not only from this hand, but in the whole world, like an opened seal in the eternal center. Anyone may reach for it; it is not only outside him, but within him. It only requires unlocking and flourishing with **Jesus Christ**, and producing a flower out of this world into the angelic world, of which we wish to speak here and show you the eternal being. <note>Reference to *The Threefold Life of Man*, chapter 3, aphorism 4 and following.</note>

My dear reader, if you wish to understand these high mysteries, you do not first need to put a university <note>original: "Academiam"</note> on your nose, nor use spectacles, nor read the books of many masters; for these are not to be sought, found, or established in the high schools alone. All that reason seeks in the arts of this world is but a dance without divine understanding. It finds nothing more than this world, and yet not even half of that; it only ever goes on searching and finally finds pride and hypocrisy in finding worldly wisdom.

Seek only the Word and heart of **God**, which became man, in the manger by the ox in the stable in the dark night. If you find that, you find Christ as the Word in the Father, together with the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, as well as eternal nature, and also the an-

<summary>Böhme argues that divine wisdom cannot be found through academic study or intellectual pride, but only through an internal spiritual connection to Christ. He characterizes his writing as a humble guide to the "light" that exists within every person.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, inner light, angelic world, academic vs spiritual knowledge, the Word, spiritual rebirth, center of being</keywords>

--- Page 246 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "angelic world" and the "lilies" mentioned on the previous page. The text moves from a warning against mockery to an explanation of how one finds the divine "Ground" through internal spiritual rebirth rather than external logic.</meta>

<page-num>(0)</page-num>
<header>(0)</header>

...angelic world and Paradise: you will find your blind reason, which has led you staggering about like a drunkard: You need not break your mind <note>original: "Gemüthe" — a central term for Böhme referring to the seat of the soul, encompassing the mind, will, and disposition</note> with lofty thoughts; you will not find the Ground <note>The underlying foundation of reality</note> with high-flown intellect and lights. Simply yield your mind and senses, with all your reason, into the love and mercy of God, so that you may be born in the center <note>original: "centro"</note> of your life from the Word and Heart of God, so that His light may shine in the light of your life, that you may be one with Him.

For Jesus Christ, the Son of God—the eternal Word in the Father, who is the radiance and the power of the light of eternity—must be born within you, O human, if you wish to know God: otherwise, you are in the dark stable, and only go about searching and groping, always seeking Christ at the right hand of God, and thinking He is far away from there. You would force your mind above the stars and seek God there, as the Sophists <note>Sophists: Böhme’s term for academic theologians who rely on intellectual logic rather than spiritual experience</note> teach, who paint God as being far away in a [distant] heaven.

But just as the Devil wants to fly above the Heart of God in his fiery torment <note>original: "Quall" — in Böhme's vocabulary, this refers to both "torment" and the "well-spring" or "quality" of action</note>, and yet remains only in the four forms of eternal nature in darkness; so it goes also for blind reason, which sits in darkness and seeks God in the darkness. If you wish to find Him, then seek Him in His working power <note>original: "Quall"</note>, which is everywhere—everything is full of God—and it shines in the darkness; God is in your dark heart, but knock at another principle <note>original: "principio"</note>, and it will be opened to you. The Holy Spirit is the key in the center <note>original: "centro"</note>. <term>The Threefold Life</term> <note>Referring to Böhme’s work "The Threefold Life of Man"</note> chapter 3, aphorism 29 and following; compare chapter 5, aphorisms 23, 26; chapter 10, aphorisms 48, 50.

It is written: the Kingdom of God consists in power, not in earthly essence: for this earthly essence is not of eternity, therefore it is also not in eternity. If you wish to contemplate heavenly being, oh, see to it that you bring a heavenly mind <note>original: "Gemüthe"</note> to the task; then the Spirit of God will surely show you heavenly being. It is far easier for the enlightened one than the earthly: the reader should not imagine it to be so difficult, for his mind [is] willingly [lost] in its own...

<meta>catchword: Under- [from "Vernunft" or "Verstand"]</meta>

<summary>This page argues that God is found within the "center" of the human heart through spiritual surrender rather than through external intellectualism. Böhme critiques theologians who place God in a distant heaven, insisting that the Holy Spirit is the internal key to experiencing the divine reality here and now.</summary>

--- Page 247 ---
<meta>This page continues Böhme’s argument that divine knowledge is inaccessible to the "Vernunfft" (natural reason) alone. He transitions from a warning against academic theology to a personal exhortation for the reader to seek the "highest mystery" within their own soul through a surrender of the self-will. This page includes citations from Böhme’s works "The Threefold Life of Man" and "Forty Questions on the Soul."</meta>

<page-num>( 0 )</page-num>
<sig>D 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: ande</meta>

**Reason** does not reach it; let man simply desist from the attempt; he only receives a **glimmer** <note>original: "Glast" — a reflection, luster, or superficial shine that lacks the true substance</note> of it, just as the **Antichrist** has and carries only a glimmer of God's Word and Christ's teaching; yet he strongly believes he has grasped the Word. *The Threefold Life*, Chapter 18, Aphorism 13.

My beloved Sir, if you wish to find the **highest Mystery**, then do not seek it in me, but in yourselves; however, not in your **reason**, which must be as if dead, and your desiring **will** must be in **God**: then the willing and the doing are within you, and the Spirit of **God** leads your will into Himself. In this way, you may then well see what **God** is, and of what spirit’s child this hand is, and from which spirit it writes.

And I admonish you brotherly that you do not seek it with such difficulty. You will not fathom it by searching in that way, even though you are known and loved by God, and this [writing] is given to you as a **guiding rule**: yet I have no power outside of myself to give it to you. Only follow my advice and go out from your heavy seeking in **reason**, and enter into the **Will of God**, into **God's Spirit**; and cast away the outward reason; then your will is **God's Will**, and **God's Spirit** will seek you within yourself.

And if He finds your will in Him, He reveals Himself in your will as if in His own property: for if you release your will, then it is His; for He is everything, and when He moves, you proceed; for you have divine power: in everything that you then search out, He is within it, and thus nothing is hidden from Him: so you see in His light, and are His.

Let no fear terrify you; there is nothing that can take this away except your <term>imagination</term> <note>In Böhme’s thought, "imagination" is the soul’s power to "form" itself or focus its desire on something; it is the creative or destructive "imaging" of the heart</note>; do not let that into your will, and you will work God's wonders in His Spirit, and recognize me as a brother in Him; otherwise, I shall indeed be **mute** to you <note>Meaning the author's words will remain silent or unintelligible to the reader's soul</note>, I tell you this with good intentions. *40 Questions on the Soul*, Question 1, Aphorism 35.

Not everyone will understand my writings according to my intent, indeed, perhaps not even one; but everyone receives according to his **gift**, for his own improvement; one more than the...

<summary>Böhme explains that divine truth is found through internal spiritual surrender rather than intellectual inquiry, warning that "imagination" is the only obstacle to experiencing God's presence. He emphasizes that his writings can only be understood if the reader’s will is aligned with the Spirit of God.</summary>
<keywords>Reason, Will of God, Mystery, Imagination, Antichrist, Threefold Life of Man, Forty Questions on the Soul, Internal Revelation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the mystical union between the human will and the Divine Spirit. The previous page ended with the idea that when a person surrenders their self-will, God’s Will begins to operate through them as His own property.</meta>

<page-num>(0)</page-num>

->**others;**<- according to how the Spirit holds its property within him; <note>The text suggests that the Holy Spirit adapts its presence based on the individual's spiritual state.</note> for the Spirit of God is also often subject to the spirits of men who wish well, and [He] sees what man desires, so that his good is not hindered, but that everywhere God’s desiring and Will might be done: For the Spirit that is born out of the **Soul-Fire** from God’s **Gentleness** <note>original: "Sanftmuth"</note> and Being, that is also the **Holy Spirit**, who dwells in the divine property and takes His being from the divine property.

What is it now within us that is foreign, so that we cannot see God? It is this world and the Devil in God’s **Wrath** <note>original: "Zorn"</note> that [prevents us from] seeing with God’s eyes; otherwise there is no hindrance.

Now, if someone says, "I see nothing divine," let him consider that for him, **flesh and blood** together with the Devil’s cunning is a hindrance and a cover; often, it is because he wishes to see God in his **pride**, for his own honor; and often, because he is filled and blinded by earthly malice. Let him walk in **Christ’s footsteps**, and enter into a new life, and yield himself under the **Cross of Christ**, and desire only the entrance of Christ, through Christ’s death and **descent into hell** <note>original: "Höllenfahrt"</note>, to the Father; what would it matter [then]? He would see the Father and his Savior Christ, with the Holy Spirit. *On the Incarnation of Christ*, Part 2, Chapter 1, Aphorism 5 and following. See <note>original: "Vid."</note> *The Signature of All Things*, Chapter 6, Aphorism 47.

Should it happen that these writings of ours are read, and that the reader might not immediately grasp and understand them; because such a foundation (which, however, through the <term>Light of Nature|The innate capacity for divine understanding present in all created things</term>, as well as being in complete agreement and grounded with Scripture) has for a long time been almost obscure, and yet, through divine grace, is given to **foolish simplicity** <note>original: "albern Einfalt" — Böhme often contrasts simple, uneducated faith with academic arrogance</note>: Therefore, may the reader not reject this according to the custom of the evil world, but look upon the foundation of the **Practice** <note>original: "Practica" — the lived, spiritual application of these mystical ideas</note> indicated therein, and commit himself to it, and pray to GOD for light and understanding. Thus he will finally understand our foundation well, and it will be and become very dear and pleasant to him.

<summary>Böhme explains that the Holy Spirit dwells within the human soul once the self-will is surrendered, though worldly pride and the "Light of Nature" remain hidden to those blinded by malice. He encourages readers to persist in studying his difficult "Practica" through prayer rather than rejecting it.</summary>
<keywords>Holy Spirit, Soul-Fire, Gentleness, Wrath of God, Light of Nature, Practice, Jacob Böhme, humility, divine vision</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the argument from the previous page regarding the inner spiritual state required to perceive divine truth. It transitions from explaining how the Holy Spirit works within a surrendered soul to a stern warning against those who approach these mystical writings with pride or purely intellectual curiosity.</meta>

<page-num>(0)</page-num>
<sig>)(4</sig>

But for the proud and those who think themselves clever—yet know nothing—whose belly is their god <note>A reference to Philippians 3:19, describing those preoccupied with worldly appetites rather than spiritual truth.</note>, who cling only to the beast of the **Babylonian Harlot** <note>In Böhme’s theology, "Babylon" represents the "confused" and corrupt institutional churches that persecute true spiritual insight.</note> and drink of her poison, and willfully choose to remain in blindness and in the **Devil’s fish-trap**: we have written nothing. Instead, we have placed a strong lock before the understanding of folly with the spirit of our **knowledge** <note>original: "Erkäntnuß"; this refers to "Gnosis" or experiential divine insight rather than mere factual learning.</note>, so that our meaning cannot be grasped by them. For they are not worthy of it, since they willfully serve **Satan** and are not God’s children.

However, we wish to be clearly and thoroughly understood by the **children of God**, and we heartfully desire to share the knowledge given to us by God, because the time for such **revelation** is born. So let each person see how they judge; each will receive their reward for it. *Preface to the Mysterium Magnum, Aphorisms 14, 16.*

I do not require calves <note>A metaphor for dull-witted or spiritually immature people.</note> for the understanding of my writings, but rather good, enlightened eyes; to others, they are completely mute, no matter how clever those people may be. *Mysterium Magnum, chapter 33, aphorism 36.*

Though we might seem mute to some in this sharp explanation, and be a stumbling block or an offense, in that someone might say we use strange, unusual speech in our foundation: we say with truth before God’s eyes that we have no other way to give it (than as it has been given to us in the **mind of Christ**): Whoever is of Christ will well understand it; for the other scoffers and pseudo-intellectuals, who have **reason** as their master, we have written nothing. *The Election of Grace, chapter 13, aphorism 16.* See <note>original: "Vid."</note> the apology against **Balthasar Tilke** <note>Balthasar Tilke (1560–1627) was a critic of Böhme who attacked his "lay" theology.</note>, aphorisms 388 and 532. Second apology against Tilke, aphorisms 294 and 299. See the *Considerations on Esaias Stiefel's booklet* <note>Esaias Stiefel (1561–1627) was a controversial German mystic; Böhme wrote a critique of his views on the perfection of the soul.</note>, aphorism 158.

It is written: **The natural man understands nothing of what is of the Spirit**, nor of the mystery of the Kingdom of God; it is foolishness to him, and he cannot grasp it. Therefore, I wish to have exhorted the **Christian lover of mysteries**, if he wishes to set himself upon these high...

[...holy] writings and read, research, and understand them; so that he does not read from the outside with sharp speculation and contemplation, for he will otherwise remain only in the external figurative ground and obtain nothing more than a painted color from it. For one’s own reason, without God’s light, cannot reach the **Ground** <term>original: "Grund"; in Böhme's thought, this refers to the ultimate essence or "Abyss" of God from which all reality emerges</term>; it is not at all possible for it, however high and subtle the **intellect** <note>original Latin: "Ingenium"; refers to natural talent or mental sharpness</note> might be. It only grasps a reflection in a mirror-glass, because Christ speaks: *Without me you can do nothing.* Also: *He is the Light of the World, and the Life of Men.* <note>References to John 15:5 and John 8:12.</note>

<margin>☩ |</margin> If one wishes to search the Divine Ground as **Divine Revelation**, he should first consider for what purpose he desires to know such things; whether he also desires to practice that which he might obtain, and to apply it for God’s glory and his neighbor’s welfare. Whether he also desires to die to earthly things and his own will, and to live in that which he seeks and desires, and to become one Spirit with Him.

<margin>|</margin> If he does not have such an intention—so that, should God reveal Himself to him with His mystery, he would want to be one Spirit and will with Him, and surrender himself entirely to God, so that <margin>|</margin> God’s Spirit may do through and with him what He will, and that God may be his knowing, willing, and working—then he is not yet <margin>|</margin> fit for such knowledge and understanding. For many seek only mystery so that they may be held in high and great esteem before the world, only for their own benefit; but they do not reach such a Ground where the Spirit searches all things, yes, even the depths of the Godhead, as it stands written. <note>A reference to 1 Corinthians 2:10.</note>

<margin>|</margin> It must only be a completely **composed** <term>original: "gelassen"; a central mystical concept meaning "yielded," "resigned," or "stilled" of one's own ego</term> person and a surrendered <margin>|</margin> will, in which God Himself searches and works, which constantly presses toward God in composed humility and surrender, <margin>|</margin> and seeks nothing else than his eternal fatherland and to serve his neighbor therewith. Then it may well be achieved; he must do it with the working of repentance and earnest [prayer...] <note>The page ends mid-sentence; the catchword "bet" in the original German likely leads to "beten" (to pray).</note>

[...pray] to begin, so that the understanding might be opened to him from within; then the internal will also introduce itself into the external.

<margin>1</margin>
When he reads such writings and yet cannot understand, he should not quickly cast them aside or deem them impossible to grasp; he should turn his mind to God and ask Him for grace and understanding, and then read again. In this way, he will see more within them, until he is finally drawn into the depths by God’s power and comes into the **supernatural, supersensual Ground** <term>original: "übernatürlicher übersinnlicher Grund"; in Jacob Böhme's philosophy, this is the ultimate, non-physical essence of reality where God exists in unity</term>, which is the eternal unity of God. There he will hear the **ineffable, actual words of God** <note>original: "unaussprechliche würckliche Worte"; these are not human speech, but the "active" or "working" creative words that form reality</note>, which will lead him back out through the Divine outflow into the coarsest matter of the earth and back in again to God. Then the Spirit of God searches all things through him and with him, and thus he is truly taught and driven by God. *Key, Preface, Aphorisms 1, 5.* <margin>1</margin> —

<margin>1</margin>
I indicate what the earthly and heavenly **Mystery** <term>original: "Mysterium"; the hidden spiritual reality behind physical appearances</term> is: namely, the holy and pure element and its outflow with the four elements and the **stars** <note>original: "Gestirne"; refers to the astrological and spiritual influences of the heavens on the material world</note>. I indicate how the Divine powers have flowed out from the heavenly and become visible, and what the inner ground of all beings is, and what Time and Eternity are.

<margin>1</margin>
Lord **Primarius** <note>original: "Herr Primar."; a title for a head pastor or superintendent. Böhme was frequently persecuted by the local clergy for his writings</note>, I truly believe that you understand nothing of this, for it is not everyone’s gift, but only for those to whom God gives it. But when you say that I wish to search out the Godhead thereby, and you call it "devilish," you bring your own ignorance to light, showing that you understand nothing of the **Book of Nature** <term>original: "Buch der Natur"; the concept that God's wisdom can be read in the creation itself, just as in the Bible</term> and that you do not even read the New Testament. For St. Paul says in 1 Corinthians 2: *The Spirit searches all things, even the depths of the Godhead.* It is not human ability, but God’s Spirit that performs such searching through the human spirit; yet you call it devilish. If I were to speak with you regarding the secret of nature, you would likely understand very little of it; but I would show you my book, *The Day-Spring* <note>original: "Morgenröthe"; Böhme's first book, *Aurora*, which was confiscated by city authorities</note>, in all things, if you [not...]

<meta>This page continues the defense of the author’s mystical insights against academic and ecclesiastical critics. ->(0)<-

...[were] not such a zealous, angry man that one could speak with you; but with such reviling you only hinder God’s gift and make yourselves unworthy. *Apologia* <note>original: "Apo. wider Richtern"; Jacob Böhme’s written defense against Balthasar Richter, the Primate of Görlitz who persecuted him for his writings.</note> against Richter, p. 343.

How does one contemplate God’s eternal counsel in divine **contemplation** <term>original: "Anschauung"; a direct, intuitive seeing or spiritual vision</term>, since the Spirit searches all things—yes, even the depths of the Godhead—as Saint Paul says, and since it is not within human capacity (as a created being) and yet is possible? How can a person truly understand such a ground of deep unity?

**Answer.** Kind reader, this question is placed here because unenlightened reason considers it impossible to know such mysteries, since it cannot grasp them, and therefore blasphemes and attributes such things to the Devil. Thus, for the sake of love for our neighbor, we wish to explain it a little, in case some might wish to become seeing and understand the deep meaning. *Theosophical Questions*, Question 12, Aphorism 1.

Hereby we have sufficiently indicated to our own people our God-given sense and understanding, and we mean it in love; it is the highest gate which God finally unlocks for us—to whoever can understand it. But to the mockers, as long as they remain so, we have written nothing, and we seal this with the eternal Will: that no mocker shall understand it. Amen. *Theosophical Questions*, Question 12, Aphorism 13; see <note>original: "vid." for "vide"; see</note> the following aphorisms; see also the *Apologia* against Esaias Stiefel <note>Esaias Stiefel was a German sectarian leader with whom Böhme disagreed on the nature of the perfection of the soul.</note>.

It is well within my thoughts that God will have opened a little gate to **Your Grace** <note>original: "E. G." for "Eure Gnaden"; a formal address to a person of high social or ecclesiastical rank.</note> which stood firmly closed to the **oppressor** <term>original: "Treiber"; literally "driver" or "taskmaster," referring to legalistic or aggressive critics of spiritual mysticism</term>, because he sought it in artifice and in pride, and thus it was withheld from him. For he took offense at the hand of the pen <note>A reference to the author's simple status as a layman/shoemaker versus the learned critics.</note> and looked at that which is written: *My power is made perfect in the weak.* And as Christ thanks His Father that He had hidden it from the clever and wise and revealed it to the babes, and says further: *Yes, Father, for so it was well-pleasing in your sight.* *Second Epistle*, Aphorism 3; see also 4, Aphorism 19 and following.

Although I know much, and a great **revelation** <term>original: "Offenbahhrung"; the uncovering of hidden divine truths</term> has been given to me, I also know well that to all those who...
