[I] am mute [to those who] are not born of God; therefore I ask you to handle my writings wisely, and also to keep my name secret, until the dark night finally comes, as has been shown to me. *7th Epistle, Aphorism 11.*

Should my written works come into the hands of the Lord <note>This likely refers to a specific noble patron or a high-ranking official to whom Böhme is defending himself.</note>, may he look upon them only as those of a child in whom the Most High has performed His work; for there is so much within them that no reason <note>Böhme distinguishes between "Reason" (intellectual logic) and "Understanding" (spiritual insight).</note> can understand or grasp it: but to the enlightened, it is childlike and quite easy.

It will not be grasped by reason: unless reason is ignited with God's Light; otherwise there is no finding [the truth], as I would kindly remind the Lord and all who read them. Christ said: "Seek, and you shall find; knock, and it shall be opened unto you." My Father will give the Holy Spirit to those who ask Him for it. Herein lies the little Pearl <term>original: "Perlein"; a reference to the "Pearl of Great Price" from the Gospel of Matthew, symbolizing the inner kingdom of God or divine wisdom.</term> enclosed; whoever wants to have it must obtain it in this way, otherwise there is no finding, only a half-blind knowledge, like shadow-boxing <term>original: "spiegelfechten"; literally "mirror-fencing," meaning an empty, illusory exercise or fighting with shadows.</term>: in the pearl lies a living science <note>In the 17th century, "science" (Wissenschaft) meant a sure and certain knowledge or understanding.</note>, where one never needs to ask if it is true; for it is written: "They shall be taught by God." Also: "We will come to you and make our home with you." Also: "Whoever does not have the Spirit of Christ does not belong to Him." Therefore Christ says: "Seek first the kingdom of God and His righteousness," etc.

He bids us strive for it, and not sit still and wait for election <note>A reference to the theological doctrine of predestination; Böhme argues for an active spiritual life rather than passive waiting for God's choice.</note>, but to come to Him. *Matthew 11.* And to work in His vineyard; not waiting to be driven, but coming willingly. *17th Epistle, Aphorism 10 and following.*

I am ready, should the mind not find enough ground in my writings to find rest, provided that [the questions] are sent to me in writing, to explain them in such a way and to bring them forth from the center of all beings <term>original: "centro aller wesen"; Böhme’s term for the divine source or the heart of existence where the spiritual and material worlds meet.</term> that I hope the mind shall be satisfied; although it does not depend on research alone, for no research grasps the pearl without God's light; a repentant, humble mind is required for it, which [submits] itself to God's grace...

...surrenders **entirely** and lets go/ so that it seeks nothing and wills nothing/ except only God's love and mercy; in that person the bright **Morning Star** finally rises, so that the mind finds such a **pearl** <term>original: "perlein"; a reference to the "Pearl of Great Price" from the Gospel of Matthew, symbolizing the inner kingdom of God or the discovery of divine wisdom.</term> in which soul and body rejoice. For it is said: *No one can snatch my sheep out of my hand.*

I exhort and beg you, for the sake of eternal salvation, to take heed of the pearl that God grants us. For a time will come when it will be sought and be found precious; do not let yourself be driven by a storm-wind, but only look upon it rightly, and pray to GOD the Most High that He would open the **door of knowledge** [Erkäntniß], without which no one will understand my writings.

For **they** go beyond **starry reason** <term>original: "gestirnte Vernunft"; in Böhme’s thought, this is the human intellect which is influenced by the stars and the physical macrocosm, but cannot reach the divine on its own.</term>/ they grasp and embrace the **divine births**/ for this reason there must also be an identical spirit/ that wishes to understand them rightly: no speculation reaches them/ unless the mind is enlightened by **GOD**: the way to such a discovery has been very faithfully shown to the seeking reader. <note>original: "18.ep. aph. 7. seqq. vid. 47. epist. aph. 30. 35." But if the soul wishes to search its own star/ as the ***mysterium magnum*** <term>Latin for "Great Mystery"; the chaos or potentiality from which God creates all things.</term>/ then it must first entirely surrender all its power and Will to divine love and grace/ and first become as a child/ and through great **Repentance** turn itself to its **center**/ and wish to do nothing/ except what the spirit of God wishes to search through it. <meta>This page continues the introduction to Jacob Böhme’s mystical theology. ->❁ (0) ❁<-

...read/ they are not written for such swine / but for children / who are to possess the Kingdom of God / for I have written them for myself / and for the seeker / and not for the clever-wise of this world. *The Three Principles of Divine Essence*, ch. 15, aph. 65.

Since we are then writing of eternity / to fulfill you in your will regarding the soul / which is our purpose here in God’s spirit and will; so we first wish to show you the ground <note>original: "Grund." In Böhme's thought, this refers to the underlying foundation or essence of a thing.</note> of the soul / and also its origin / and to open your eyes well / so that you may be rid of your difficult searching: for you have pursued this into your old age / as I understand / and have not found the deep mystery in the Spirit.

But because it is God’s will / that you should know and recognize it / and such a reward for your labor shall be given to you: so see to it / that you receive it rightly / and do not throw the pearls <note>A reference to Matthew 7:6; Böhme frequently uses the "pearl" as a metaphor for divine wisdom.</note> any further before the swine / who are not worthy of them / and shall not be worthy in eternity. For what will be revealed to you herein / belongs to God's children: therefore be faithful / and act therewith according to the spirit / and not according to reason <note>original: "Vernunft." Böhme distinguishes between human logical reasoning (which is limited) and divine "understanding" or "spirit."</note>: for it is so subtle / that it does not suffer the earthly nature born in greed, pride, or self-glory: although you are children / only see to whom you pour oil / for to some it is a poison; let them seek for themselves / as you have done: but give bread to the children / so they may eat / and praise our Father in Heaven / for to that end it is also given to you. *Forty Questions on the Soul*, q. 1.

We will therefore speak / so that we will be well understood by the children / and keep a full mouth for the children: For it is not good to write / as one knows not who the reader will be.

But to the godless we say / that they are of the devil / and shall have no part in our writings: we enclose them with a wall / and a firm lock / so that they are blind / and do not know our spirit; for we do not want to place the serpent there / our will has gone out from them / therefore they shall not know us / and even if they carry us in their hands / there is a firm seal upon it. *Forty Questions on the Soul*, q. 6, aph. 7, 8; cf. q. 38.

We have not written this work for the irrational beasts / which have an external human form / but...

<summary>Böhme explains that his writings on the origin of the soul are a divine reward for the seeker's long labor, intended only for "God's children" while remaining spiritually "sealed" and invisible to the godless and the prideful.</summary>

...[whose] images—in the spirit—are evil and wild beasts, which reveals and represents itself in their characteristics. Rather, [these writings are] for the human image—for those who sprout <note>original: "ausgrünen." Böhme uses this botanical term to describe the organic growth of the soul into its divine form.</note> from the animal image into their own human image <note>original: "Bildniß." This refers to the "imago Dei" or the spiritual form a person takes on.</note>, those who belong in God's Kingdom and who desire to live and grow in the human image, in the true human. These people are often and greatly hindered by the contrary life, and thus remain stuck in a mixed life, laboring in anxiety toward the birth of the holy life. For them, we have written these writings, and we tell them that they should not consider it impossible to recognize or to know such mysteries. *Preface to the Six Points. See The Signature of All Things, chapter 12, aphorism 37. Apology 2 against Tilke <note>Balthasar Tilke was a contemporary critic of Böhme; this refers to Böhme’s written defense against him.</note>, aphorism 35 and following, aphorism 52. 10th Epistle, aphorism 2 and following, aphorism 12 and following.*

This I have written to the hungry and thirsty hearts seeking Christ's fountain, as to my fellow members in the spirit of Christ. But for the scoffers, I have not written it: for they have their book within themselves, with which they drive the children of Christ under the cross, and—against their will—they must be servants to the children of Christ, even if they do not understand this. *Preface to the little book Of Regeneration, aphorism 3.*

I recognize the great effort you devote to this, and I thank my God, who has sent me a person in this world with whom I may speak of God's kingdom; for otherwise almost everyone chooses to be blind and mad, such that I dare not even open my mouth.

I hear scoffers who mingle among us, whom I care little about, for I know whose spirit's children they are, and I wish them my own recognition; they would then cease their ways.

Regarding N.’s <note>A placeholder for a specific name, likely an indiscreet student or acquaintance of Böhme.</note> copying of my future writings, I do not know how best to guard myself against him, for he does not remain silent; and I often hear loose people interpreting my current writings, which I believe comes from him, for I have shown them to no one else. Since he is almost entirely worldly, and born only from the school of this world, we might be poorly protected.

One should also first [give] the pearls, seeing as they are so precious...

[they] should not be scattered on the path: but wait for another time / until they become common / so that the oppressor <note>original: "treiber." This literally means "driver" or "taskmaster," referring to the worldly or institutional authorities who persecute those with heterodox spiritual insights.</note> does not swallow them.

He might well be allowed to transcribe it / but not the first time / but after it has been transcribed once / So that the oppressor may not break it. *4th Epistle, aphorism 33 and following. See <note>original: "vid." (Latin vide).</note> 5th Epistle, aphorism 3 and following.*

My writing is to you / and I remind you out of high consideration and Christian good intention / that you should not give my writings into everyone's hands / for they are not everyone's food.

Also, one must not throw the pearl onto the path / so that it is trodden underfoot / whereby the worthy name of God might be blasphemed · for I recognize very well what Satan has in mind / but it has been shown to me / how his undertaking must fail.

Although a heavy darkness is to be feared / in which time the light in the hearts of men will first truly bloom <note>original: "grünen." Literally "to turn green" or "to sprout," a favorite botanical metaphor of Böhme's for spiritual growth.</note> / when they shall stand in great tribulation and abandonment: then they will seek the Lord / and he will let himself be found.

My writings do not serve the full belly / but for a hungry stomach / they belong to the children of the mystery <note>original: "geheimnuß." This refers to the hidden divine wisdom or "Sophia."</note> / especially since in them many noble pearls lie enclosed and also openly.

I have also not written them for the unlearned <note>original: "idioten." In the 17th century, this was a technical term derived from the Greek *idiōtēs*, meaning a common person or layperson without formal academic or clerical training, rather than a modern slur.</note> / or for the clever / but for myself / and for those to whom God will give the understanding of them. *7th Epistle, aphorism 1, 5.*

Reveal my little pearls only in the hearts of the wise / whom you recognize / that they love God; for the others it is currently of no use / for many seek nothing other than evil and pride / as well as false cleverness / so that he may show himself off; therefore I ask you to act wisely; many a person accepts such things with joy / but he has an evil root / he intends to become pious / but he lets the devil hold him / and afterwards becomes a mocker of such revelations: This I say to you well-meaningly / not from my own delusion / but from present true knowledge <note>original: "erkäntnuß." A deep, experiential spiritual insight rather than mere factual learning.</note>. *7th Epistle, aphorism 9.*

<meta>This page continues Böhme's discourse on the intended audience for his mystical works. If anything in my writings seems too difficult and incomprehensible / I ask you only to take note of it / I wish to present it simply <note>original: "kindisch." Literally "childishly," meaning in a plain, humble manner suitable for a child’s understanding rather than academic complexity.</note> / so that it may be understood. For the clever and the satisfied / for the high and the self-righteous / who can walk on their own / and are already rich / for those I have written nothing / but for the children and the babes / who suck at the mother’s breast and are learning to walk.

Let he who can understand it, understand it: but he who cannot / let him leave it un-slandered and un-criticized / I have written nothing for him. I have written for myself.

But if a brother were needy / and asked me for water / I would give him to drink / he will experience what I have given him / if the Lord grants him the drink. *12th Epistle, aphorism 78 and following; see <note>original: "vid." ## CHAPTER XVI.

### One should not mock Jacob Böhme and his writings.

If you do not understand it / then leave this book in peace / and judge neither the cold nor the warm within it / for you are too much a prisoner in <term>Saturn</term> <note>original: "Saturno." In Böhme’s mystical system, Saturn represents the "astral" influence of contraction, darkness, and coldness; a person "captured in Saturn" is spiritually rigid and lacks the "light" needed to perceive divine mysteries.</note> / and are no <term>philosopher</term> <note>original: "philosophus." Böhme uses this term in the Hermetic sense: someone who studies the secret workings of God within nature through spiritual illumination.</note> in this world. Simply cease your judging / or you will receive an evil reward / for which I wish to have warned you plainly / wait until the next life / when the gates of heaven will be opened to you / then you will also understand it. *Aurora, chapter 11, aphorism 3.*

Because the Spirit of Heaven allows the divine form to be known / I cannot cease to write in this way / and I leave it to him who wishes it so. Although the devil might stir up mockers and despisers regarding this / I care nothing for that / the lovely revelation of God is enough for me / they may mock as long as they like / until they experience it with eternal shame / then the source of remorse will surely gnaw at them.

I have also not ascended into heaven / and seen such things with fleshly eyes / much less has anyone told me: for even if an angel came and told me, I could not grasp it, much less believe it, without the illumination of God.

For I would always remain in doubt / whether it was a good angel by God’s command, since the devil can also disguise himself in the form of an angel to lead people astray / *2 Corinthians 11:14; Aurora, chapter 12, aphorism 116.*

You live between Heaven and Hell / into whichever you sow / in that same thing you shall also reap / and that same thing shall be your food for eternity. If you sow mockery and contempt / then you shall reap mockery and contempt / and that will be your food. Therefore, child of man, watch yourself / and do not trust too much in worldly wisdom / it is blind and is born blind. *ibid. aphorism 120.*

Do you think / we are mad once again? Our foolishness will stand before your eyes at the Last Judgment / to that we appeal. *Three Principles, chapter 25, aphorism 35.*

We wish to have the reader faithfully warned / that he should not seek our meaning in the wisdom of this world / but in the light of eternal nature / to which we also wish to have directed him / as into the new rebirth / into the life of *Christ*; otherwise we are mute and misunderstood to him / and outside of this, he might as well leave these writings unmastered / or he will eat the food of the first <term>center</term> <note>original: "centri." In Böhme's system, the "first center" refers to the harsh, dark, and consuming qualities of nature (the "fire-world") that exist before the "light" of God’s love transforms them.</note> / and his mockery will gnaw at him in the fire of his own life.

We would gladly grant him the light / for the sake of which this hand has thus written down the deep mysteries / not for its own advantage / which it already possesses / but for the sake of the lilies <note>Böhme uses the "lily" as a symbol for the coming spiritual renewal or "reformation" of the world, a time of peace and divine flowering.</note> and the angelic world.

Take careful note here / you will see / what you have not seen since the heavy fall of *Adam*: and consider also / what this signifies / and what appears herewith: Do not step in the footsteps of the proud Pharisees / who crucified *Christ* / and remained blind to the light; otherwise, the same will happen to you.

Also, do not look at the hand holding this pen / it can do nothing / but look at the <term>Center</term> / from which the light shines. It does not shine from this hand alone / but in the whole world as an opened seal in the eternal <term>Center</term> / everyone may reach for it / it is not only outside of them / but within them / and it means only to unfold <note>original: "auffschlicken." Likely an archaic term for a bud unfolding or a sprout breaking through the soil.</note> / and turn green with *Jesus Christ* / and bear witness

...a flower out of this world, into the **Angelic world**, <note>original: "Englische welt." In Böhme's terminology, "English" (Englisch) refers to the state of angels, not the nationality.</note> of which we wish to speak here, and show you the eternal being. *Threefold Life, chapter 3, aphorisms 4, 7.*

Should this text be difficult for the reader to understand, we would nonetheless have him exhorted to be patient and to read diligently. Even if it does not seem possible for him to grasp it now, it will hereafter—when the ***Threefold Life*** of man is written about—be very useful, and only then come into its true understanding, so that he may then treasure it as a great **Jewel**. <note>original: "Kleynodt." A term often used by Böhme to describe a precious spiritual gift or insight.</note>

For the mind does not cease searching until it reaches the innermost ground, which is indicated here. But if it does not reach the ground, it sinks into the abyss <note>original: "grund." Böhme uses "Ground" to describe the deep foundation of reality, and "Unground" or abyss for the source before all existence.</note> and cannot grasp it; then doubt, unbelief, and contempt enter the mind. For this reason, we would have the reader warned not to trifle with these high **Mysteries**. Otherwise, the Spirit of God is blasphemed.

And it happens to the mind as it did to **Lucifer**: when he saw the greatest mysteries of the Godhead standing in such humility, he took offense and entered into the harsh power of fire. He wanted to rule over God through his own cleverness; God was supposed to be subject to him. He wanted to be the master of the images in nature, and for that reason, he became a devil.

For in gentleness and humility stands the kingdom of the Heavens with the angelic world, and the power of the heart of God; for the light stands in gentleness. *Threefold Life, chapter 4, aphorisms 59 ff.*

O man, consider what you judge, so that you do not fall upon the sword of the Spirit of God, and your works be blown away in the fire of wrath. For look upon the image in the **Revelation**, <note>A reference to the Book of Revelation 1:16, where a sharp two-edged sword comes out of the mouth of Christ.</note> which carries the sword in its mouth: it truly signifies the Spirit of God, of whom Christ says: when he comes, he will judge the world concerning sin, concerning righteousness, and concerning judgment.

Concerning sin, because they live in **hypocrisy** <note>original: "gleißnerey." Literally "shining-ness," meaning a false, outward religious appearance that lacks inner truth.</note> and do not obey the Spirit of God, nor believe in him—that he might reveal heavenly wonders within them. Instead, they remain under the wrath in the first <term>center</term> <note>The "first center" in Böhme’s mystical physics represents the dark, contracting, and fiery forces of nature that have not yet been transformed by the light of divine love.</note>, refusing to be **reborn**, but instead revealing only wonders in wrath in vain hypocrisy.

And concerning righteousness, Christ says: "that I go to the Fa—"

Concerning slandering, we would have warned the reader—as dear to him as his soul and eternity are—for he will not touch us, but rather the fierce wrath of God within himself. But me, who has been the cause [of this writing], he may indeed touch; for I stand without his touching in the bonds of Christ. <note>original: "Christi banden."</note> I would, however, have him exhorted in love to show himself as a brother in Christ, and where he is able through divine gifts, to make an even clearer explanation. ...[let] us deal with one another as brothers and members <note>Böhme uses the biblical metaphor of the "Body of Christ," where all believers are limbs or members of a single body.</note>: with mockery we can accomplish nothing good: we only confuse people—namely, the world—and the simple-hearted person therewith.

If you have Christ's Spirit, as you let yourselves think, then meet me and my brothers in the gentle love and humility of Jesus Christ; show me your love in the Spirit of Christ, who came in great humility to us poor lost children of Eve <note>original: "Eua-kindern." A common term in this period for all of humanity, inherited from the Fall of Adam and Eve.</note> to seek and to make blessed that which was lost; if you are children of that same spirit, then be so faithfully, with gentleness, love, and friendliness, with modest speech, and cast away the horns of mockery, so that I may recognize that you are my brother; then I will love you, should God give you more gifts than me: if not, I will nonetheless rejoice with you in weakness, and delight in what God gives.

Do not be so wild against the precious revelation which God has finally granted us; if you read it correctly from the start, it has a very noble, high-precious origin <note>original: "urstand." This is a central term in Böhme’s philosophy, referring to the primordial foundation or the "original state" of being before the physical world.</note> and beginning, which reaches beyond all reason—yes, beyond the outer world, and beyond the light of outer Nature—why do you rage against the Most High?

I admonish you in a Christian manner: see to what you do, so that the wrath of the Lord does not seize you and God curses you. I tell you, I will be innocent of your souls if you awaken that [wrath].

See what happened with Elijah, and also Korah, Dathan, and Abiram <note>Old Testament figures who were consumed by divine fire or swallowed by the earth for rebelling against God’s appointed leaders (Numbers 16).</note> in the wilderness; I tell you, as much as is known to me in the Lord, it might happen to you and many others, for it is now a wondrous time, not known or recognized by all. The Lord has sent his Spirit of Zeal <note>original: "Eiffer-Geist." This refers to the "jealous" or "zealous" aspect of God's Spirit that demands purity and executes judgment.</note>; a wondrous time is at the door, which you shall experience if you live. <term>Apologia 2, against Tilke, aphorisms 198, 202.</term> <note>Böhme is citing his own "Apologia" written against the pastor Balthasar Tilke, who had attacked his writings.</note>

My dear brothers! Do not be hostile to me because of my knowledge <note>original: "Wissenschaft." In the 17th century, this did not mean "science" in the modern sense, but rather a deep, systematic spiritual understanding or "knowing."</note>; for I, being who I am, did not know beforehand what I have written to you; I thought I was writing for myself alone, and it turned out this way without my awareness: I tell you in good faith, if it is not your gift to understand it, then leave it be for me, for I understand well what I have written.

If anyone can understand it and desires it, I will gladly grant it to him.

<header>(0)</header>

grant [it]; where however [this is] not the case, and he does not desire it, because he does not understand it, so let him only not sin by mocking and blaspheming against God, or a Severity <note>original: "Ernst." In Böhme's theology, the "Ernst" or "Severity" of God refers to the divine judgment or the wrathful, consuming side of the deity that follows the rejection of light.</note> will follow thereafter, of which you will want to know nothing, nor be able to, in such a course. Just allow me that I work in that into which I have been set.

Yet my opponent <note>original: "gegensatz." Böhme uses this to refer to his theological adversary, Balthasar Tilke, a Silesian nobleman and literalist critic who challenged Böhme's "Way to Christ."</note> says, "we can think or will nothing good without God, nor know anything of God outside of Him." I also say thus: that I know nothing of God outside of God; my knowledge is in Him, and stands in His Power.

Why then does he attack me for this, that God knows within me, so that I should reveal the way to Him to you? For I am nothing, and He is everything: He who can understand it, let him understand it; but he who cannot, let him leave it be. With such objections you will neither ground nor understand my writings.

I see your spirit much better than you understand what kind of counter-objections you make, yet I cannot find that my opponent understands anything fundamental in my concept; rather, it is all only a wild, harsh-envious quality <term>Eigenschaft: a technical term for Böhme meaning the "inner property" or "disposition" of a person's spirit</term>, entirely mocking, so that I pity his misery more—that he is so blind to it—than I am eager to answer him. <note>original: "apol. 2. wieder Tilcken. aph. 308. 312." Refers to Böhme's work *Apologia II* (Second Defense) against Balthasar Tilke, specifically aphorisms 308 and 312.</note>

Such things I have not wanted to hide from you, dear hearts and brothers in Christ, you who read my writings and make use of them, upon consideration of my opponent; and I admonish you brotherly, as well as my opponent, that you would hold the Holy Scripture against my writings, but seek nothing else in the Scripture than the Fatherly Heart of Love of **JESUS CHRIST**; thus you will surely find from what spirit I have written.

But he who does not wish to do this, let him leave my writings in peace; for I have written nothing for him; I have written them for myself alone, without consideration of how things should go with them; nor do I know how it happens that they spread so. For I have run after no one with them, and I wonder alongside you at what the Most High does.

Take note of it, and become seeing, for the day is dawning; if you learn to understand my writings rightly, you will be [freed] from all...

delivered from all strife / and learn to know yourselves; however, the letter <note>original: "buchstabe." ### To the Reader.

Dear reader, do not consider this an uncertain fiction / it is the true ground / and contains within it the whole Holy Scripture: for the Book of the Life of Jesus Christ is painted clearly before [your] eyes therein / just as it was recognized by the author himself / for it has been his own process <term>Proces: In Böhme’s mystical vocabulary, a "process" is the lived experience of spiritual transformation and the alchemical-like refinement of the soul.</term>: He gives you the best that he has; may God give the growth! A heavy judgment has been recognized upon the mocker of this. Let him be warned. Little Book of True Repentance. aphorism 52.

Because Richter’s pasquil <note>original: "paßqvüll." A satirical lampoon or abusive pamphlet. This refers to the printed attacks by Gregorius Richter, the head pastor (Primarius) of Görlitz.</note> says: There were as many blasphemies / as there are lines in these little books / so it is obvious / that he blasphemes and slanders the Holy Spirit / for he calls repentance and prayer a blasphemy; for the little book deals solely with repentance and the new rebirth.

Dear Lord judging Judge <note>Böhme is punning on the surname of his adversary, Gregorius Richter, whose name literally means "Judge" in German.</note> / where will you go out or where will you go in / when the supreme Judge Christ / whom you shame in his members <note>i.e., his fellow believers or the "body of Christ."</note> / shall appear in His wrath / and will judge the judging Judge / and will Himself be the law and the judgment? This Judge will not look upon a Head Pastor <note>original: "Primar." Short for Pastor Primarius, Richter's title.</note> or his following / which he draws to himself / nor any king / prince or lord or world / according to their possessed power / nor their laws / but will judge the life and test the same in His fire; where then shall your shaming and slandering remain / which you have driven against Christ in His members: It stands written / whoever blasphemes the Holy Spirit / he has no forgiveness eternally: what then will you answer Him / that you have knowingly blasphemed His gift / and so miserably persecuted the children of Christ / when Christ shall say: what you have done to these my least brothers / that you have done to me; what answer will you give to that?

You despise me / because I am a layman / and have not come from the high schools <note>i.e., universities.</note> with my science, and you "fie" <note>an expression of disgust or contempt</note> at my gifts / which I have nonetheless received from God / as a noble—

<summary>Böhme defends his work against the "pasquil" of Pastor Gregorius Richter, arguing that his writings on repentance are divinely inspired and warning that those who judge him will face Christ's ultimate judgment. He highlights the conflict between academic "high schools" and his own status as an inspired layman.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, Gregorius Richter, repentance, rebirth, Holy Spirit, blasphemy, layman vs. scholar, spiritual judgment, Balthasar Tilke</keywords>

Böhme is responding to Richter’s public condemnations of his writings, transitioning from a general defense of his "Little Book of True Repentance" to a personal plea regarding the source of his divine inspiration.</meta>

->(0)<-

<note>original: "ist geschenck..." This sentence completes a thought from the previous page regarding Böhme's spiritual insight.</note> is a gift / which is dearer to me / than the whole world. Do not mock my handiwork with your "shame on you" / and call my gift a stench: how might it go for you if such blasphemy were to awaken in body and soul / and your "shame," with which you spit upon the gift of the Holy Spirit, awoke within you / and were infected with God's wrath, so that the world became too narrow for you? What would you then want to give / to have not done such a thing? The spirit which now delights you / so that you may spend your mood in blaspheming and slandering me / might yet become a hellish fire for you / and step into [your] conscience; where will you then remain? You draw from me tears and earnest prayer against your blasphemy / in that you accuse me entirely with untruth: See to it that my tears do not become glowing coals upon your head; I have prayed much to God for you / that God would enlighten you / and give you the Truth to recognize / but you become ever worse with blaspheming: I will be innocent of you / whether God’s wrath shall touch you.

Dear Lord Head Pastor <note>original: "Primarius." This refers to Gregorius Richter, the chief clergyman of Görlitz and Böhme's primary antagonist.</note> / why do you despise me / because I am a layman <note>original: "laye." Böhme was a shoemaker by trade, and his lack of formal university theological training was a central point of Richter's attacks.</note> / and have high divine and natural knowledge? Do you think that the Holy Spirit is bound to your schools? Did Christ, our Master and teacher, say / my Father will give the Holy Spirit to all who ask him for it? Likewise / ask, and you shall receive / knock / and it shall be opened unto you. I have received my gift through earnest asking and knocking / and have tasted such a thing / that I would not give it for all the world's honor and goods / nor can I ever deny it. Why do you attribute the gift of God to the devil? Look back into the world <note>Meaning: Look back through history.</note> / at what simple people God has often used for his work; who were Abel, Seth, Enoch, and Noah? Who were the patriarchs? Sheep-herders /

<meta>catchword: which</meta>

<summary>Böhme defends his spiritual writings as a divine gift, warning his persecutor, Pastor Richter, that slandering the Holy Spirit carries eternal consequences. He asserts that divine knowledge is not restricted to university-trained scholars but is given to those who "knock" and "ask," citing biblical examples of simple laborers chosen by God.</summary>

who also were no Doctors <note>original: "Doctores." Refers to those with university degrees in theology.</note>; who was Moses? 
a shepherd; who was David? a shepherd; who 
were the prophets, especially Elisha and Habakkuk? 
simple, godly people who went about with husbandry <term>ackerwerck: agricultural labor or farming</term>; who was Mary, the mother of Christ? a poor, godly, 
orphaned maiden. Who was Christ’s foster-father 
in his childhood? a carpenter; who were Christ’s 
Apostles? Altogether poor, simple craftsmen, 
such as fishermen and the like. Why did Christ 
not also reject them because they had not studied? Did he 
also sneer at them because they were craftsmen, 
as you do? What has the nobility of Christians been from the beginning? 
humility and the fear of God. Where then lies your great 
boast in the scriptures regarding your art <term>kunst: here meaning formal academic training or human skill</term>? Show it to me; 
St. Paul was a scholar <note>original: "schriftgelehrter." Literally "one learned in the scriptures," or a Scribe.</note>, but when he 
became a Christian, he first had to become a 
fool in his art and wisdom, so that Divine 
Wisdom might find a place within him. Why do you then boast of your art, 
if you call yourself a Christian? Do you not 
know that in Christ we are all but one? That is 
Christ in us all.

Since Christ now teaches and loves us, why 
do you curse us then, and call Christ in us a "stench"? 
It does not look well, as if the evil 
spirit were in you, who cannot endure Christ, 
nor does he like to see people do repentance and pray, 
because you call Repentance <term>Busse: the spiritual process of turning one's heart back to God</term> and Prayer a "stench," 
which you cannot deny, because you say 
there is a blasphemy in every line, and you forbid 
the people to read it, under eternal punishment. Apology against 
Richter <note>original: "apol. wieder Richtern." This refers to Böhme’s written defense against the accusations of the head pastor Gregorius Richter.</note> pages 38, 440.

<summary>Böhme argues that God historically chooses simple craftsmen and laborers over scholars, citing biblical figures like David and the Apostles to justify his own status as an uneducated layman. He accuses his primary critic, Gregorius Richter, of acting under an evil spirit for labeling his devotional writings on prayer and repentance as "stench" and blasphemy.</summary>

--- Page 274 ---
<meta>This page continues Jacob Böhme’s direct address to Gregorius Richter, the head pastor of Görlitz. The previous page ended with Böhme accusing Richter of calling the practice of repentance and prayer a "stench."</meta>

Lord Senior Minister <note>original: "Herr Primar." Refers to Gregorius Richter, the Chief Pastor of Görlitz and Böhme’s primary adversary.</note>, at that time you shall give an account to God regarding your blasphemies; for it will be required of you how you have gone before your trusted little sheep with a good life and sound doctrine. There you will find a heap of blasphemers whom you have led astray and made to blaspheme; you have poured your poison and blasphemy into them and sprinkled them with it. Where will you turn then, when their blood shall be required at your hands? Just look at your fruits; the whole city blasphemes because of your outpouring over a little sheep of Christ <note>Böhme frequently refers to himself as a "little sheep" (Schäflein) to contrast his humble status with the "wolf-like" behavior he attributed to the established clergy.</note>, and they think in their simplicity they are doing right because you tell them to do so. 

Where will you turn when this little sheep of Christ stands before your eyes at the Final Judgment, and your listeners, whom you have made into blasphemers, shall say: "Look, is this not the one whom we once held for a fool and a mocking example, and we regarded his life as a disgrace; how is he now numbered among the children of Christ? O, we fools and madmen, we have missed the right way." (Wisdom of Solomon 5:3). At that time they will all curse you for leading them to such blasphemy and shame. Lord Pastor, be warned by the life of our Lord Jesus Christ; it is time to desist, or you will see what has been told to you here.

Lord Senior Minister, in your lampoon <term>Pasqvill: a public satirical or defamatory pamphlet, often posted in a public place.</term> you call upon the righteous Judge to wake up and protect the Majesty of God. What if the judgment were to pass over you? Because you persecute Christ in his members <note>Referring to the "Body of Christ," the idea that any harm done to a believer is harm done to Christ himself.</note>, you have called for the sword yourself. Just look into your heart; you depict to the congregation <meta>catchword: community</meta>

<summary>Böhme warns Gregorius Richter of the divine judgment he faces for leading his congregation into blasphemy and persecuting a "sheep of Christ." He cites the Wisdom of Solomon to illustrate how the townspeople will eventually regret mocking the godly.</summary>

<keywords>Gregorius Richter, Final Judgment, blasphemy, Wisdom of Solomon, Pasquil, persecution, Görlitz, shepherd and sheep</keywords>

--- Page 275 ---
<meta>This page continues Jacob Böhme’s defense against Gregorius Richter. Böhme is responding to a defamatory pamphlet (lampoon) Richter wrote against him, challenging the pastor to find actual evidence of heresy in Böhme’s writings.</meta>

[you] intend to cast a mist before their eyes, so that they should believe you, that your lampoon <term>Pasqvill: a public satirical or defamatory writing, often used in the 17th century for character assassination.</term> is right.

Lord Senior Minister <note>original: "Herr Primar." A title for the head pastor of a city’s main church.</note>, why do you not take the evidence from the printed booklet? Perhaps you have none, or it does not suit your taste; for earnest repentance <note>original: "Busse." This refers to the spiritual labor of turning one's heart back to God, a central theme in Böhme's mysticism.</note> causes pain to the flesh; it surely does not suit your taste.

But the people who read my little books and hold your lampoon against them, they shall judge between us and judge rightly: See to it that the filth and burrs which you throw at me do not remain clinging to you. I will no longer remain as silent as I have been: to desist would be good; peace is a good land. <note>Böhme cites his own work: "apologia against Richter," pages 340 and 347.</note>

Lord Senior Minister, what will you answer to Christ and to me when your lampoon burns in your soul? Who has given you the authority for such a judgment? Prove this from the teaching of the Apostles; now there would still be time to do penance, before I must summon you, by God’s impulse, before the earnest judgment seat of Jesus Christ! Mark what I say to you: I am a Christian in Christ; you call Christ in his members a heretic, and his power you call filth <note>original: "Dreck." Böhme uses this strong language to highlight the gravity of the pastor's perceived blasphemy.</note>. The mercy of Christ calls to you herewith once more, whether you wish to repent; if not, then I shall be innocent of you when you are brought to judgment. You have a great mountain before you over which you must climb; should Christ forsake you, then you must indeed remain eternally in this abyss.

Mark what has been told to you; it is recognized that hypocrisy counts for nothing; it must be earnest. Sinning upon grace [be-] <meta>The text breaks mid-word; the catchword "komt" indicates the word is "bekomt" (receives/obtains), continuing on the next page.</meta>

<summary>Böhme warns Pastor Richter that his defamatory "lampoon" will lead to his own spiritual ruin and summons him to repent before the final judgment of Christ. He asserts his identity as a true Christian and accuses the pastor of calling the power of Christ "filth."</summary>

Lord Senior Minister <note>original: "Herr Primar." The head pastor of the city's main church.</note>, you shall then give an account to God for your slanders; for it will be demanded of you how you led your trusted little sheep with a good life and sound doctrine. There, you will find a heap of blasphemers whom you have led astray and made into mockers; you have poured your poison and blasphemy into them and sprinkled them with it. Where will you go then, when their blood is demanded from your hands? <note>A reference to the biblical warning in Ezekiel 3:18, where a watchman is held responsible for the lives of those he fails to warn.</note> Just look at your fruits: the entire city blasphemes because of your outburst against a little sheep of Christ <note>Böhme refers to himself here as a "little sheep" (Schäflein), emphasizing his perceived innocence and humility against Richter's institutional power.</note>, and in their simplicity, they think they are doing right because you command them to do so. Where will you go when this little sheep of Christ stands before your eyes at the Last Judgment, and your listeners—whom you have made into blasphemers—shall say: "Look, is this not the one whom we once held for a fool and a scornful example, and whose life we considered a disgrace? How is he now counted among the children of Christ?" O we fools and madmen, we have missed the right way. <note>original: "Sap. 5. 3." This refers to the Wisdom of Solomon 5:3-5, a book of the Apocrypha, which describes the regret of the unrighteous when they see the salvation of those they once mocked.</note> Then they will all curse you for leading them to such blasphemy and shame. Lord P., be warned by the life of our Lord Jesus Christ; it is time to desist, or you will see what has been said to you here.

Lord Senior Minister, in your lampoon <term>Pasqvill: a public satirical or defamatory writing used for character assassination.</term> you call upon the righteous Judge to wake up and protect the Majesty of God. What if the judgment were to fall upon you? Since you persecute Christ in his members <note>A reference to the theological concept that all believers make up the "Body of Christ" (1 Corinthians 12:27); thus, attacking a believer is seen as attacking Christ himself.</note>, you have summoned the sword yourself. Just look at your heart; you paint the [congregation...] <note>The sentence breaks off here; the catchword "meind" indicates the word "Gemeinde" (congregation or community) follows on the next page.</note>

<summary>Jacob Böhme warns Pastor Gregorius Richter that he will be held spiritually accountable for leading his congregation into sinful mockery and "poisonous" slander. He cites the Wisdom of Solomon to illustrate the future regret Richter and his followers will face at the Last Judgment.</summary>

--- Page 278 ---
<meta>This page concludes the "Apology against Richter," where Jacob Böhme delivers a final, stern warning to Gregorius Richter. It follows the previous page’s challenge for Richter to provide proof for his slanderous attacks.</meta>

<page-num>(0)</page-num>
<header>❁ (0) ❁</header>

Wrath approaches; the hour is near; let it be said to you: you will have no protector and assistance there, such as you draw to yourself here and rely upon. You must look me in the eye and give an account for your wickedness and slander <note>original: "Boßheit und lästerung."</note>. Christ in me and in all members of Christ summons you before the Judgment of Christ. What then shall I say to you regarding the stains of shame <note>original: "schandflecke."</note> that you have hung upon me on Earth?

O Lord Senior Minister <note>original: "Herr Primar." The title for the head pastor of the city's primary church, Gregorius Richter.</note>. I beg you as a member of my soul, turn back while the door of grace <note>original: "Gnaden-thür."</note> still stands open, before the great chasm between me and you is closed (Luke 16:26) <note>The original text cites "Luc. 16. 62.," but this is likely a printer's error for Luke 16:26, which describes the "great gulf" between heaven and hell.</note>. It truly ill suits a High Priest, who should carry the Divine Word of Christ's love in his mouth, that he instead takes filth <term>Filth: "Koth" in the original, meaning mud or excrement; Böhme uses this visceral image to describe the Senior Minister's insults.</term> into his mouth and splashes Christ’s words—which he is supposed to teach—with filth, and sprays such filth into the hearts and souls of the people and poisons them. For where filth is, the Holy Spirit is surely not in the mouth, but rather Satan. The Christian congregation might well open their eyes and notice what they are being taught; God's punishment follows. Let it be said to you, dear brothers! A time will shortly come when you will see what I am saying here.
